> **Functional-first framing rule:** A10 ensures that the three-layer architecture is narrated explicitly in order: functional (what the system does, via verb-noun `action def` and abstract functional role definitions) -> logical (how functions are partitioned into implementation-agnostic components with defined interfaces, via `abstract part def` + `flow`/ports) -> physical (concrete part selections that fulfill logical roles, via `part def` with physical attributes). `abstract part def ToastingSystem` and its specializations are the **logical** layer — they define component boundaries and interfaces without committing to a physical solution. Narrative cells must use verb-noun convention (e.g., "transform bread into toast," "apply thermal energy") when describing functions, and must distinguish logical structure (with interfaces) from physical implementation (concrete part selection).

## DL-012 | 2026-09-25 | Cross-WP | Phase 2 pilot: all 13 notebooks use Pattern B; multi-fragment convention adopted

Path: Handled by ACE — implementing Z's explicit design directives; architecture revision logged

Decision: All 13 construct-introducing notebooks use Pattern B (SysML string fragments) for
their construction zones. Pattern A (Editor API) is fully deferred. The multi-fragment convention
is adopted: one named fragment variable per element = one future `editor.add_*()` call. Each
fragment is in its own code cell, printed immediately, with a narration markdown cell after it.
`TOASTER_INCREMENT` is assembled from the fragment variables at the end of the construction zone
and equals new declarations for that notebook only (not the full cumulative model).

Rationale: Phase 2 pilot (Ch3/nb02) probed `add_calc_def` with `inputs` kwarg → TypeError.
Further probing confirmed: `add_attribute` produces fixed binding (not `default =`), and
`add_action_def` / `add_member(kind='action def')` produce bare declarations only. Together
with D-004–D-010 (already confirmed), the API cannot produce any of the 13 notebook constructs
in their correct form. Z's directions (verbatim):

1. "we can do this but then we need to make sure all the gaps are well documented as issues.
   each location we encounter this issue needs its own comment markdown, linking to issue in this
   repo, linking to issue in opensysml and these much carry exact citation to the spec so its
   clear we're only asking for the spec to be implemented not extraneous feature requests."
2. "be careful to avoid mega strings. don't do the whole increment in one call or even one cell.
   you need to do increments do them in smaller chunks. always needs to be inspectable & intuitive."
3. "code should be factored the same way it would be if we had the api calls we wanted."

New gaps confirmed and filed (Phase 2 pilot 2026-09-25):

- D-011: `attribute default =` modifier — toaster#16 / OpenSysML#603 (KerML §8.4.1)
- D-012: `calc def` body (inputs + return expression) — toaster#17 / OpenSysML#604 (SysML v2 §7.16)
- D-013: `action def` body (params, sequencing, nested actions) — toaster#18 / OpenSysML#605 (SysML v2 §7.15, §7.20)

All 5 upstream OpenSysML issues filed: OpenSysML#601 (flow/D-009), #602 (state/D-010), #603 (attr
default/D-011), #604 (calc def body/D-012), #605 (action def body/D-013). DEFERRED.md updated.
All 4 skills updated with confirmed issue numbers and multi-fragment convention. Plan updated.

## DL-011 | 2026-09-25 | Cross-WP | Declarative construction architecture: notebooks ARE the build

Path: Handled by ACE

Decision: Adopt declarative construction architecture. Cell-02 in each of 13 construct-introducing notebooks declares the SysML increment (via Editor API or SysML string); the cumulative `.sysml` files become generated checkpoints. The `scripts/check_construction.py --check` script verifies consistency. Full plan in `decisions/declarative-construction-plan.md`.

Rationale: Z's verbatim direction — "ideally someone who pulls this repo constructs the sysml v2 model, they are not pulling an existing one. we're teaching engineering here." The hybrid architecture (Pattern A = Editor API for ~8 construct kinds; Pattern B = SysML string for 5 gap constructs pending OpenSysML#595–599) is the only viable path given current implementation gaps. The scope is 13 notebooks, not all 31.

Key findings from ACE review of the plan (B-ACE-3 critical):
- `editor.apply()` returns the FULL model (all existing + new members), not just the new member.
  Probed: base = `package P { part def X; }`, after `add_part_def('Y')` → `"package P { part def X; \n    part def Y;\n}"`.
  This invalidated the "reconstruct from TOASTER_INCREMENT concatenation" design for the verification script.
  Fix: `check_construction.py` verifies consistency only (run construction cells, check they parse, compare
  last Pattern A TOASTER_INCREMENT per chapter to committed fixture). Does NOT reconstruct from scratch.
- Pattern A TOASTER_INCREMENT = full cumulative model. Pattern B TOASTER_INCREMENT = new SysML fragment only.
  The two patterns are not interchangeable. The verification script must handle both separately.
- Ch1 has no ch00-cumulative; Pattern A cells within Ch1 chain on the prior notebook's TOASTER_INCREMENT.

ACE review findings table: 4 blocking + 5 minor, all corrected in `decisions/declarative-construction-plan.md`.
Gap construct notes already added to 5 affected notebooks; upstream issues filed (OpenSysML#595–599);
DEFERRED.md entries D-004–D-008 present with cross-references.

## DL-010 | 2026-09-25 | Cross-WP | ISQ/SI unit typing + B+ hybrid-systems interface contract

Path: Handled by ACE

Decision: (1) All 8 model files updated to use `ISQ::PowerValue`, `ISQ::DurationValue`, and `ISQ::EnergyValue` (Option A — ISQ for all physical attributes from ch01). `resistance` and `gauge` in ch06 stay `Real` (structural placeholders, not simulation quantities). (2) Ch7/nb01 updated with `BINDING` dict (B+ option): explicit sympy↔SysML attribute mapping driving `lambdify` argument order, with narration framing the dict as the data dictionary for the continuous dynamics.

Rationale: Z identified the toaster as a hybrid system (discrete state machine + continuous dynamics) and directed an explicit interface contract between SysML continuous-valued fields and their sympy symbol definitions. Z chose Option A (ISQ everywhere) over Option B (calc def only) and Option C (defer), framing the unit-typing decision itself as substantive engineering judgment. Z's framing: the BINDING dict is "essentially a data dictionary that also determines the basis for how the SysML model tells an engineer to interpret data generated by simulations."

Probe findings (2026-09-25):
- `attribute x : ISQ::DurationValue default = 120.0 [SI::s]` parses + allows override: ok=True
- `= VALUE [SI::UNIT]` without `default` creates a fixed binding (cannot override): avoided
- Mixed ISQ+Real in `calc def` (efficiency stays Real): ok=True
- `model.eval()` with ISQ-typed params requires unit-annotated literals (`[SI::W]`, `[SI::s]`)
- Return type is `opensysml.values.Quantity`; `.magnitude` extracts numeric value; `float()` fails
- `model.eval("ToasterDemo::DeliveredEnergy(800.0 [SI::W], 120.0 [SI::s], 0.7)").magnitude = 67200.0`
- IDE language server reports conformance errors on ISQ literals; opensysml v0.9.0 runtime accepts them

Files changed:
- `models/ch01-ch08-cumulative.sysml` (all 8): ISQ imports + typed attributes + unit-annotated values
- `chapters/ch07-execution/01-calc-energy.ipynb`: BINDING dict + narration + updated model.eval call
- `.claude/skills/sysml-v2-toaster-model/SKILL.md`: ISQ unit typing section added

## DL-006 | 2026-09-25 | WP-5 | SA-6 adaptation: use verify_satisfaction() in Ch8; verify_constraint(engine="check") unusable

Path: Handled by ACE
Decision: Ch8 uses `model.verify_satisfaction()` instead of `model.verify_constraint(name, subject, engine="check")` as specified in SA-6 and the Chapter plan.
Rationale: WP-5 probe confirmed `verify_constraint(engine="check")` returns "not covered" for any constraint — it does not evaluate attribute overrides and cannot discriminate nominal (holds=True) from slow (holds=False). `verify_satisfaction()` evaluates `assert satisfy` declarations correctly and returns `Verdict(holds=True/False, element=...)` for each. This is the right API for Ch8's learning outcome (SA-6 specifies "bounded model checking: opensysml `check` engine only" — the spirit is "no external model checkers," which `verify_satisfaction()` satisfies). Ch9 previously planned to introduce `verify_satisfaction()`; Ch9 is not yet built, so the introduction point moves to Ch8 with no impact on prior chapters. SA-6 skill entry to be updated to note this probe result. Z pre-approved minor corroborated fixes.

Path: Handled by ACE
Decision: Remove `java` from `check_tool_versions()`. Only `dot` (Graphviz) is required.

## DL-002 | 2026-09-25 | WP-1 | Diagram pipeline: Python-generated DOT selected

Path: Handled by ACE
Decision: opensysml v0.9.0 provides no native DOT rendering. Use `model.query()` → `model_to_dot()` → Graphviz `dot -Tsvg` for all relationship/hierarchy diagrams.
Rationale: WP-1 probe confirmed: `sysml-grpc` is a gRPC server with no render flags; Python API has only `model.render_document()` (document queries, not diagrams). Python-generated DOT gives full control, is traceable to the model, and is testable. Probe test `tests/test_diagram_probe.py` passes: DOT generated, SVG rendered. Documented in `sysml-diagrams` skill.

## DL-005 | 2026-09-25 | Ch2-Ch3 | Requirement anatomy + verification def

Path: Handled by ACE (Z directed during context compaction)
Decision: Add (1) `doc` rationale to `TimelyToast` in Ch2; (2) new Ch3-nb04 notebook introducing `verification def TimelyToastTest` (§7.24); log `VerificationMethodKind` metadata gap as D-004 / toaster#19 / OpenSysML#608; add A10 Systems Architect archetype to AGENTS.md.
Rationale: Brian Douglas Part 4 specifies the 3-part requirement anatomy (description, rationale, verification method). The `doc` comment (§7.21.2) carries informal text in the requirement def; `verification def` (§7.24) is the spec construct for verification cases. `verify` must target a requirement *usage* (not a definition) — confirmed by probe (`ok=False`, error: "satisfy target must be a requirement usage, found requirementDef") — so the verification def is placed in Ch3 where `timely : TimelyToast` is already defined, not Ch2. The `VerificationMethodKind` metadata construct is a gap in OpenSysML v0.9.0.

Reasoning: (a) Blocking, per user-testing, is a non-executing cell, a multi-sentence concept statement, a seam that names Tall or "three worlds" or does not address the seam in behavior, a passing negative control, or a broken cumulative model; the ACE re-ran every cell and none holds, and both learners could point concretely to model text, loading tool and printed result, so the seam is behaviorally addressed. (b) The world labels: whether they name the lens (1.10 first clause) is DL-028's undecided question and is not reopened; the learner evidence bears on 1.10's second clause and P4, whose test is whether removal makes understanding harder — the Novice reports the tags obscure the connection and the Practitioner read the sentence as complete without them, so removal makes understanding easier, the opposite of earning a place. DL-003 found the same confusion under the pre-Foundations regime (naming three worlds was then a PASS criterion); under 1.10 the remedy reverses from defining the abbreviations to dropping them. The notebook conforms to toaster-recipe line 143 and tutorial-style-guide line 59, which is the recorded recipe-versus-rule contradiction; removing a required element from the recipe requires Z, and that decision is already parked, so nothing is escalated anew. (c) Lint: DL-028 applied F6 and P5 to lint design — a check establishes only the prohibition it is defined to cover, and the world labels were explicitly left to the recipe rewrite; widening now would decide that question by regex, "(E)" is unmatchable regardless, and the lint-plus-behavioral-judgment split is exactly what this dry run exercised and confirmed. (d) Probes: at v0.9.0 the abstract increment loads alone, isAbstract is recorded, and a direct usage of the abstract def loads without diagnostic (legal SysML), so the caveat is correct only as a statement about the Editor API (D-004); check_construction.py lines 11-13, 196, 212 consume TOASTER_INCREMENT. (e) Mechanism observation: neither persona flagged the index.md/conclusion.md layer-vocabulary defects; the learner checklist has no such step and the layer-auditor role is the tool for that, so the two evaluation workflows catch disjoint things as intended.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no (P4 and 1.10 second clause are written for lens vocabulary in learner content; DL-028 is applied as a decision, not extended).
Provenance: AGENTS.md 1.10; DL-028 (Pass 4 inputs, not decided); DL-029; DL-003 (WP-2 checkpoint, pre-Foundations "all Tall seams name three worlds" as a PASS criterion); DEFERRED.md D-004 (toaster#9 / OpenSysML#595, verified); glossary/lint_rules.toml `tall-named` regex (verified); `.claude/skills/toaster-recipe/SKILL.md` lines 103-106, 143, 156; `.claude/skills/tutorial-style-guide/SKILL.md` line 59; `.claude/skills/sysml-v2-toaster-model/SKILL.md` line 156; scripts/check_construction.py lines 11-13, 196, 212; models/ch01-cumulative.sysml header; learner reports `decisions/dryrun/pass3-learner-novice-ch01.md`, `decisions/dryrun/pass3-learner-practitioner-ch01.md`; ACE execution and probes of nb01 and nb02, 2026-09-27.

## DL-054 | 2026-09-28 | PHASE0-DIAGRAM-STUDY-000 | COMPLETE: orchestrator-protocol extended with a plan-driven, non-chapter work path so Phase 0 of the diagram-study plan runs through the existing builder/reviewer/ACE harness

Path: Z-directed (Z asked, in chat, after approving `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-diagram-study-phase0-plan.md` and choosing subagent-driven execution: "make sure to abide by our existing subagent driven architecture. you update previous or make new skills and role scopes to ensure this work remains checked by our multiagent harness with orchestration and escalation processes already defined")
Decision: PENDING. Investigated first (per skill-editor Step 1): `.claude/agents/{orchestrator,builder,reviewer,ace}.md` are already role-agnostic — blast zone, acceptance criteria and model all come from the per-task work contract, not from hardcoded chapter/notebook assumptions — and need no edit. `decisions/work-contract-template.md` and `decisions/task-states.md` are likewise already fully generic. The one real gap: `.claude/skills/orchestrator-protocol/SKILL.md` is entirely the legacy WP-0..WP-9 chapter-build table, and its escalation triggers are chapter/construct-specific (opensysml construct unavailable, figure quality gates, an SA challenged) — it has no path for a `docs/superpowers/plans/*.md`-driven engineering task (this one touches `scripts/diagram_study/` and `decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures/`, neither a chapter nor a skill), and no escalation triggers for tooling/trade-study work. Intended change: add one new, additive section to `orchestrator-protocol/SKILL.md` — "Plan-driven non-chapter work" — describing that a `docs/superpowers/plans/*.md` task (or a sensible grouping of its steps) becomes one work contract via the existing generic mechanism (blast zone and acceptance criteria copied from the plan document's own task/step text, review loop and merge gate unchanged), plus a short list of escalation triggers specific to this class of work (a pinned external tool cannot be (re)provisioned and no recorded workaround exists; a builder or reviewer produces a real finding that contradicts an assumption the approved spec or plan states as settled — e.g. a mutation-control verdict; a scope question the plan did not anticipate, such as whether to add back a tool the plan named out of scope). The existing WP-0..WP-9 table is not touched (still governs chapter work) and AGENTS.md Part 2's legacy authority matrix is not touched (chapter-specific; not applicable here since blast zone is already stated per-contract, not via a pre-registered matrix entry).
Revert record (verbatim current file, `.claude/skills/orchestrator-protocol/SKILL.md`, commit `8d6a9692bc3a6bcb422487f939a12b4ae7fc9d68`, 57 lines): `git show 8d6a9692bc3a6bcb422487f939a12b4ae7fc9d68:.claude/skills/orchestrator-protocol/SKILL.md` — sections "WBS quick reference" (lines 8-21), "Review loop protocol" (23-32), "Escalation triggers" (34-41), "Chapter build dependency (file-based model strategy)" (43-50), "What A1 must never do" (52-57), unchanged by this edit.
Principles applied: skill-editor Step 1 (pre-edit DL gate, written before any edit) and the "Z-directed alignment pass" clause (Z's direction, quoted above, satisfies Step 2's escalate-to-Z gates for the edit this entry names — the change does add a capability not in the original WP-0 design, which would otherwise require escalation); Step 3 minimal-change rule (purely additive, no existing section altered, well under the 20% threshold since 0% of existing content changes).
Reasoning: the multiagent harness's own design goal (AGENTS.md Part 1's role-agnostic artifacts, Pass 1 Handoff item 2) is that duties and artifacts are named generically so roles and work shapes can grow without rewriting the mechanism; the actual gap here is narrow (one skill's worked-example table and escalation list assume chapter work) and the fix is additive, not a redesign.
Determined: yes. Post-edit check done: re-read the new section plus the two adjacent sections ("Chapter build dependency" above it, "What A1 must never do" below it) — no adjacent rule weakened or contradicted (the orchestrator still never edits files, authors content, or escalates directly to Z; the new section explicitly routes its escalations through the ACE, consistent with that). No conflict with AGENTS.md Part 1 or a confirmed glossary definition: the edit is process/orchestration only, adds no new term and touches no layer or definitional content. Added one section ("Plan-driven non-chapter work", ~13 lines) between "Chapter build dependency" and "What A1 must never do"; 0% of existing content changed (purely additive), well inside the minimal-change rule.
Extension: yes — adds a capability (a non-chapter, plan-driven work path) beyond the original WP-0 design; covered by Z's direct instruction per the Z-directed alignment pass clause, not an ACE ruling.

## DL-055 | 2026-09-28 | PHASE0-T6 | SysMLD/sysml2d at pinned commit 1af88250 cannot index real Ch5/Ch7 content; Task 6 is DONE with that finding, no SVGs, tool not patched, aliases not altered

Path: Handled by ACE
Decision: Task 6 is done. Its deliverable is the finding that SysMLD/sysml2d (pinned 1af88250) fails render and validate --strict on both real fixtures because its textual indexer pops a scope frame on any line beginning with "}" while pushing frames only for a fixed keyword set, so doc-comment and assert-constraint braces in `ApplyHeat` pop the `ToasterDemo` package frame before `Toaster` or `Cycle` is indexed. The two intent files (aliases verbatim from the fixtures) and the failing-command logs are committed as evidence; no SVG is produced; the pinned checkout is not patched; aliases are not rewritten to the indexer's truncated names; `render --no-strict` may be run only as a labelled diagnostic, never as the capability result. Task 7 records SysMLD as "mutation control not runnable: baseline render failed" and Task 8 reports the original staleness finding as neither confirmed nor refuted on real content. No `DEFERRED.md` entry (not a tutorial construct or dependency); any upstream issue draft goes to Z before filing.
Principles applied: P5 (do not paper over; a construct or tool is described as working only after it has been run); P1 (record the evidence and the residual uncertainty rather than manufacture a verdict); F4 (the model is the authority; a hand-authored intent file that no longer corresponds to it is not a rendering of it); plan Review Focus items 1, 3 and 4 and Task 4 Step 5 (real-content failures are matrix findings, not blockers; no vacuous mutation pass; alias drift is the integrity risk); spec decisions 1 and 6 (SysMLD not adopted, reasons preserved; match the original study's rigor, which modified no renderer source); task-states.md blocked criteria (no checkable unblock_when exists inside Phase 0).
Reasoning: (1) Reproduced independently: on `models/ch05-cumulative.sysml` the pinned indexer reports `ToasterDemo::Toaster` False and `Toaster` True; on ch07 `ToasterDemo::Cycle` False; the source shows the unconditional pop on "}". So the plan's Task 6 assumption that an unresolved alias is a copy error is contradicted, and the failure is the tool's. (2) Patching changes the object of comparison and breaks the pinned-version constraint; false aliases manufacture a pass; `--no-strict` skips model correspondence entirely, so none of the three yields the deliverable the plan defines. (3) The plan already classifies a tool's real-content failure as a finding and says not to treat it as blocking; the spec included SysMLD for low-cost comparison completeness and asks that rejection reasons be preserved, which this finding does. (4) Hence done, with amended acceptance (intent files, logs, probe record; no SVGs) and the downstream Task 7/8 handling above.
Determined: yes.
Extension: yes, narrow. P5 and the plan's failure-as-finding rule applied to a throwaway third-party trade-study tool rather than to tutorial content; the register-and-issue mechanics of AGENTS.md 1.9 are held not to apply because the tool is not a tutorial construct or dependency. Z's prior acceptance of the original study's SysMLD staleness result as a recorded reason for non-adoption (spec decision 1) is the closest precedent.
Provenance: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-diagram-survey-design.md` (decisions 1 and 6; Phase 0 Method "comparison completeness ... marginal cost ... small"); `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-diagram-study-phase0-plan.md` (Global Constraints pinned versions; Review Focus items 1, 3, 4; Task 4 Step 5; Task 6 Step 4; Task 7 Step 7); `/Users/z/Downloads/toaster/diagram-study/report.md` lines 36 ("minimal textual index") and 81 ("No renderer source was modified"); `/private/tmp/toaster-diagram-study/sysml2d/src/sysmld/model_index.py` and `src/sysmld/cli.py:115,170` and `src/sysmld/render_svg.py:39-46` at commit `1af88250` (clean checkout, `git status` empty); ACE probe on `models/ch05-cumulative.sysml` and `models/ch07-cumulative.sysml`; `decisions/task-states.md` (blocked criteria; escalated → previous state on ACE ruling); AGENTS.md Part 1 §1.9 (gap-tracking rule scope; probe before you assert).

**Addendum (orchestrator, 2026-09-28, after Task 6's independent review):** the review confirmed this ruling's core substance (all 12 aliases across both intent files are real, correctly-qualified elements in the fixture source; the indexer bug is real, independently reproduced line-by-line against the pinned source; ch07 fails on the indexer bug alone, a clean single-cause case) but found the ch05 case is not as clean as this entry's reasoning states. Two of ch05's six SYSMLD-REF-002 errors are NOT from the indexer bug: they're unaliased connector-endpoint ids (`control--heating--src`/`control--heating--tgt`) that `compose` auto-generates and that the original toy-fixture study's own worked example DID alias (`toaster-mech-composed.json`), but this plan's Task 6 JSON (copied verbatim by the builder, not the builder's error) omitted. So for ch05 specifically, "the failure is the tool's" is only partly established: the indexer bug alone is sufficient to block ch05 too (confirmed: even with the two missing aliases added in a scratch copy, the unpatched indexer still fails all 6), so the bottom-line ruling (done, no SVG, tool not patched) is unaffected — but the claim "if the indexer were fixed, ch05 would also render cleanly" is NOT verified and should not be asserted. Task 6's committed evidence (`sysmld-indexer-probe.json`'s "conclusion" field, and the commit message's causal summary) is being corrected to state this precisely, per push-back to the builder. Task 7/8 should describe ch07 as the clean, single-cause confirmation of the indexer bug, and ch05 as blocked by the same bug with an additional, separately-caused authoring gap in the intent file's endpoint aliases (a plan defect, not a fixture or tool-adoption question) — not claim ch05 would work absent the indexer bug.

## DL-056 | 2026-09-28 | PHASE0-T8 | Phase 0 (real-fixture diagram-tool trade-study rerun) complete; capability matrix at decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures.md; Phase 1 remains unspecced pending Z's review

Path: Handled by ACE (recorded by the orchestrator; this entry closes out already-approved Phase 0 work, not a new judgment)
Decision: Phase 0 is done. Its deliverable, `decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures.md`, compiles Tasks 1-7's real-fixture evidence (`models/ch05-08-cumulative.sysml`, four common-model tool pipelines plus SysMLD) into a capability matrix mirroring the original toy-fixture study's `report.md` structure. The original toy-fixture study's tool-capability conclusions hold for three render pipelines — OpenSysML's two render forms (PlantUML, DOT/Graphviz) and sysml-toolkit, all 18 of 18 non-pilot real-fixture render attempts succeeding — but two of the four candidate tools, the OMG pilot (a common-model tool) and SysMLD (an intent-file tool), cannot be exercised on real content at all, for two different, independently confirmed reasons: the pilot fails all 6 of 6 real render attempts on a genuine capability gap (qualified-name allocate targets, untested by the toy fixture, e.g. `models/ch05-cumulative.sysml:111`); SysMLD/sysml2d (pinned `1af88250`) cannot index real content at all under a confirmed indexer bug (DL-055 and its addendum), so its original toy-fixture mutation-control ("silent staleness") finding is neither confirmed nor refuted on real content — it now fails loudly and early (strict-invalid, exit 1) rather than silently, a different and arguably better-behaved failure mode, but the underlying structural risk (a hand-authored intent file drifting from the model, uncaught) remains untested. OpenSysML separately reproduces its known port-collapse limitation on real content (zero port-name tokens drawn anywhere in Ch5's interconnection view); sysml-toolkit does not share it. Phase 1 (the per-chapter diagram survey) and any implementation work remain explicitly out of scope and unspecced, pending Z's review of these findings, per the spec's own Non-goals.
Principles applied: P5 (a tool or view type is described as working only after being run against real content, not the toy fixture); P1 (record evidence and residual uncertainty rather than manufacture a verdict); F4 (the model is the authority; SysMLD's intent-file approach and OpenSysML's tree-view element choice are both judged against what the real model actually contains, not assumed).
Reasoning: Tasks 1-7 established, and Task 8 independently re-verified every cited number, exit code and containment claim against the committed evidence files (`provisioning-report.json`, `real-fixture-results.json`, `view-type-probe.json`, `mutation-control-results.json`, `sysmld-indexer-probe.json`, the four `sysmld-*.log` files) and against the fixture source directly (diffs, line-level containment checks), across four rounds of independent review (the deliverable's own compilation went through one FAIL and two rounds of precise, evidence-backed correction before a clean PASS — see the deliverable's own commit history on `phase0/task8-deliverable`). The toy-fixture study's qualitative tool-capability picture holds only for the three tools that can render real content at all; the tree view's byte-identical renders across Ch5-Ch8 are a correct consequence of rendering `Toaster`'s own unchanged declaration block from that root (a "wrong root chosen" scope boundary, not a recursion-depth limitation) — reaching `heatGen`/`rated` would require choosing a different root element entirely, untested here. Mutation-control reruns were scoped to `ch05-interconnection` and `ch07-state` only, matching Task 7's own scope, not every successful baseline. This changes what Phase 1's tool recommendations can lean on: the SysMLD mutation-control question specifically is not answered by this phase, only reframed, and the pilot's real-content viability is untested pending an upstream fix or workaround.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — this entry records completion of already-scoped, already-approved Phase 0 work; it opens no new judgment call.
Provenance: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-diagram-survey-design.md` (Phase 0 method, Non-goals); `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-diagram-study-phase0-plan.md` (Task 8, Review Focus item 5); `decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures.md`; `decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures/evidence/*.json` and `*.log` (Tasks 1, 4, 5, 6, 7); `decisions/log.md` DL-055 and its addendum.

## DL-057 | 2026-09-29 | PHASE0-DIAGRAM-STRATEGY-000 | COMPLETE: renamed `render_sysmld` to `render_interconnection` (it never used real SysMLD) and corrected `sysml-diagrams`' per-view-type tool table to match what Phase 0 actually proved

Path: Z-directed (Z approved, in chat, the "Approach C: prove-then-use" tool-selection policy and said "yes, you can apply these corrections" for exactly the two items below, within the ongoing brainstorming design conversation for the diagram-generation strategy that follows Phase 0)
Decision: PENDING.
1. **Rename** `src/toaster/render.py::render_sysmld` to `render_interconnection` (same implementation, same signature — it already never depended on the real SysMLD tool; its own docstring already says so: "SysMLD CLI is a pilot visualizer and not a build dependency; this function implements the same semantics using DOT/Graphviz"). The old name is misleading now that Phase 0 (DL-055) found the real SysMLD tool cannot index real content at all — keeping a same-named in-house function invites exactly the kind of confusion `decisions/next-passes.md` §5 already shows happened once (that backlog note lists "SysMLD port-level interconnection" as "in force by decision," conflating the real tool with this in-house reimplementation). Update all 4 references: `src/toaster/render.py` (the function itself), `chapters/ch05-architecture/03-interfaces.ipynb` (the shipped, merged notebook that calls it), `exercises/ch05/exercise.ipynb`, `tests/test_interconnection.py`.
2. **Correct** `.claude/skills/sysml-diagrams/SKILL.md`'s "Choose the renderer by the question" table and "Diagram pipeline decisions (updated SA-9)" section, which currently recommend the OMG pilot for tree/state views and (ambiguously) "SysMLD" for interconnection, and claim OpenSysML has no working render CLI at all — all three claims are now contradicted by Phase 0's real-fixture evidence (`decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures.md`; the pilot fails all 4 real fixtures; real SysMLD fails to index any real content; OpenSysML's `-render #kind:element` CLI shorthand succeeded on 16/16 real uses, further confirmed by a direct probe against Ch6's real `ApplyHeat` action during this conversation). Replace with the "prove-then-use" table: structure/tree → `model_to_dot()` (in-house, unchanged); action-flow and state → OpenSysML's `-render` CLI, `-render-form dot`; interconnection → `render_interconnection()` (in-house, renamed per item 1) or sysml-toolkit where port-box detail is pedagogically load-bearing; sequence/traceability → unchanged (DOT, no real content exists yet to exercise sequence views).
Revert record: `git show 1988ef1bd6cd758df15249896253457fb0bfb195:.claude/skills/sysml-diagrams/SKILL.md` (the skill file's content before this edit); `git show <commit before this edit>:src/toaster/render.py` for the pre-rename function.
Principles applied: skill-editor's Z-directed alignment pass clause (Z's direction, quoted above, satisfies the escalate-to-Z gate — this does remove a "must never do"-adjacent claim, i.e. it retracts previously-stated tool guidance, which would otherwise require escalation); minimal-change rule (renames and table corrections only, no restructuring).
Reasoning: `decisions/next-passes.md` §12 already flagged that every chapter re-derived so far has deferred its figure and recommended a dedicated diagram pass once the chapter sequence is stable (now true); the skill's current table would send Phase 1's survey agents and any future notebook author toward tools Phase 0 has hard evidence don't work on real content, reproducing exactly the "willy nilly" inconsistency risk Z raised in this conversation.
Determined: yes. Post-edit check done: re-read the whole skill file after the two main edits and found two adjacent sections ("Keep intermediate representations small", "Tailor like a scientific figure") still called the in-house function "SysMLD" and described a "reference-name check" that belongs to the real third-party tool this tutorial doesn't use — corrected both in the same pass so no adjacent rule contradicts the corrected table. Final grep for "SysMLD" confirms every remaining mention now correctly distinguishes the third-party tool (not used, confirmed broken on real content) from `render_interconnection()` (used, never depended on it). `uv run pytest tests/test_interconnection.py -v` passes all 9 tests after the rename (sed-renamed test function names too); full behavior unchanged, only the name and documentation.
Extension: yes — retracts and replaces previously-stated tool guidance in a skill; covered by Z's direct instruction, not an ACE ruling.
Provenance: `decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures.md` (Phase 0's capability matrix); `decisions/next-passes.md` §5, §12; `src/toaster/render.py` (read in full: `model_to_dot`, `render_dot`, `build_interconnection_intent`, `render_sysmld`, `render_action_flow` — the last is an unimplemented stub); a direct probe run in this conversation (`/private/tmp/functional-toaster-design/sysml models/ch06-cumulative.sysml -render '#action:ToasterDemo::ApplyHeat' -render-form dot`, exit 0, real DOT output) confirming action-flow rendering works on real content, which Phase 0 itself never directly tested.

## DL-058 | 2026-09-29 | DIAGRAM-STRATEGY-T4 | Qualified-name allocate ends (ToastBread::applyHeat to Toaster::heating, ch05-ch08) are language-tier non-conformance; the pilot's rejection is a correct diagnosis, not a pilot gap

Path: Handled by ACE
Decision: `allocate <Def>::<usage> to <Def>::<usage>;` at package level (ch05:111; ch06:117,150; ch07:119,173; ch08:119,173) violates KerML 1.1 Beta 2 validateSubsettingFeaturingTypes (the connector end cannot access the referenced feature) and checkConnectorTypeFeaturing (no featuring context; defaultFeaturingType null, so no implied TypeFeaturing can satisfy it). Language-tier non-conformance per DL-039; OpenSysML v0.9.0 and sysml-toolkit v0.9.1 accept it (two tool holes, DL-039 (3) pattern: tutorial-supplied always-on guard with a negative control, DEFERRED entry, drafted upstream bug reports for both tools, comment cell at the generating notebook cell; nothing filed until Z reviews). `decisions/diagram-tool-gaps.md`'s G-D001 is reworded from a pilot capability gap to a fixture defect the pilot correctly reports; the pilot's real-content rendering result is `blocked` (unblock_when: fixtures free of language-tier violations) and is not evidence against the pilot; no upstream issue to the pilot. Conformant idioms, probed: `allocate toastBread.applyHeat to heating` nested in `part def Toaster` (matches the logical-layer reading, F2/F7) or `allocate nominal.toastBread.applyHeat to nominal.heating` at package level; the model fix is follow-up work, not part of this plan or DL-058 itself — a `DEFERRED.md` entry in D-019/D-020's shape is warranted as separate, out-of-scope-here follow-up.
Principles applied: F6, heuristic 8, P5; DL-039 (confirmed extension: language conformance is defined by the spec's validation constraints, not a tool's `ok` flag) applied as a decision; F2/F7 for the recommended idiom only (guidance, not ruled).
Reasoning: (1) An allocate end is a ConnectorEnd : ReferenceUsage with an OwnedReferenceSubsetting resolved from a QualifiedName (SysML 8.2.2.13, 8.2.2.15); `ToastBread::applyHeat` resolves to a Feature, so D-019 is satisfied and does not address this case; an AllocationUsage is a ConnectionUsage to which checkConnectorTypeFeaturing applies (SysML 8.3.15.3; 8.4 p. 427). (2) validateSubsettingFeaturingTypes requires `subsettingFeature.canAccess(subsettedFeature)`; canAccess requires the referenced feature to be isFeaturedWithin one of the end's featuringTypes ({heatAllocation, Base::Anything}); neither is compatible with ToastBread or Toaster; violated at both ends; a validation constraint is a language rule (KerML 8.3.1; DL-039). (3) checkConnectorTypeFeaturing with empty featuringType requires isFeaturedWithin(null); ToastBread and Toaster have no common featuringType, so defaultFeaturingType is null and implied-relationship rule 2 forbids the rescue; corroborating. (4) Re-run on the pinned tools: pilot fails the original at both ends and renders both rewrites; toolkit and OpenSysML accept all three; the toolkit's `connectors.rs` documents a "one-hop featuring lift" with no counterpart in canAccess. (5) Heuristic 8: language tier, always on; the spec's own allocation idiom is chains from accessible usages; allocating the definitions themselves is D-019.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no.
Provenance: KerML 1.1 Beta 2 (`sysmlv2-testing/sources/local/kerml-1.1-beta2.pdf`): 8.3.1 constraint categories p. 135; canAccess 8.3.3.3.4 p. 188; isFeaturedWithin p. 190; validateSubsettingFeaturingTypes p. 204; 8.3.4.5.3 Connector checkConnectorTypeFeaturing and defaultFeaturingType pp. 214-215; implied-relationship rule 2 p. 281; 8.4.4.6.1 p. 289. SysML v2.0 formal/2026-03-02 (`sysmlv2-testing/sources/local/formal-26-03-02.pdf`): OwnedReferenceSubsetting p. 204; 8.2.2.13 ConnectorEnd p. 208; 8.2.2.15 Allocations p. 210; 8.3.15.3 AllocationUsage p. 335; 8.4 type-featuring constraints p. 427. Pilot source: `SysML-v2-Pilot-Implementation/org.omg.kerml.xtext/src/org/omg/kerml/xtext/validation/KerMLValidator.xtend` lines 222-223, 269-270, 824-832, 1004-1034; training examples `sysml/src/training/38. Allocation/`. Toolkit: `sysml-toolkit/crates/sysmlv2-model/src/check/connectors.rs` header (v0.9.1, af839f0). Probe 2026-09-29: pilot via `java -cp .../jupyter-sysml-kernel-0.62.0-all.jar .../PilotRender.java <sysml.library> <file> ToasterDemo::Toaster tree <out.svg>`; `sysmlv2 check <file> --lib <sysml.library>`; opensysml `load_from_content(src, strict=False)`. Results: original pilot exit 1 (ERROR "Must be an accessible feature (use dot notation for nesting)" line 111 cols 40, 65), toolkit exit 0, OpenSysML ok=True; nested-in-Toaster and nominal-chain variants pilot exit 0 with SVG, toolkit exit 0, OpenSysML ok=True; `ToastBread.applyHeat` variant (a definition heading a feature chain) pilot "Couldn't resolve reference to Feature". Z statements: Z-27 (two tiers), Z-12 (toolchain flags spec gaps). Prior decisions: DL-039, D-019, D-020, DL-055/DL-056. Evidence cited by the question: `decisions/diagram-study-real-fixtures/evidence/ch05-tree-pilot-emit.log`; G-D001 at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-29-diagram-generation-strategy-plan.md:627`.

## DL-059 | 2026-09-29 | ALLOCATE-FIX-T4 | DL-058 remediation complete except upstream bug drafts: `allocate-connector-end-accessibility` guard rule and D-032 land; two new, out-of-scope fixture non-conformances found and flagged, not fixed

Path: Handled by ACE (recorded by the orchestrator; this entry closes out already-scoped follow-up work from DL-058/`decisions/next-passes.md` item 23, not a new judgment call)
Decision: Task 4 of the DL-058 remediation effort (`allocate-fix/task4-gap-rule`, off `allocate-fix/impl` after Tasks 1-3 rewrote every chapter ch05-ch10 to the conformant nested/dot-chain `allocate` idiom) adds the last piece: a fourth `GapRule`, `allocate-connector-end-accessibility` (`src/toaster/conformance.py`), matching D-019/D-020's shape. It flags a single-segment (bare qualified-name) `AllocationUsage` connector end whose declaring context (the end's qualified name minus its last `::`-separated component) is neither the allocation's own owner nor a plain `Package`; a multi-segment (dot-chain) end is never flagged, since OpenSysML's own parser already proves it accessible by the time a model loads. `DEFERRED.md` `D-032` documents the gap and marks it GUARDED (unlike D-019/D-020, which remain open holes with no guard): the tutorial's own model is fixed and a regression is now caught, but the underlying OpenSysML/sysml-toolkit acceptance-without-diagnostic gap itself remains open upstream. `decisions/next-passes.md` item 23's sub-items (a) (the DEFERRED entry) and (b) (the guard rule) are now done; (c) (drafted upstream bug reports) remains open.

The algorithm was validated empirically, not merely derived: zero findings on every real cumulative model fixture (`models/ch01-cumulative.sysml` through `ch10-cumulative.sysml`, no ch09 fixture) and on `tests/test_interconnection.py`'s `FLOW_SOURCE`, `ALLOC_SOURCE` and `NESTED_SOURCE` inline fixtures; a fresh, isolated synthetic negative control (not reused from `QUALIFIED_ALLOC_AND_INTERFACE_SOURCE`, per next-passes.md item 23's own concern that fixture mixes this pattern with the unrelated DL-039 definition-level one) confirmed to trip only this rule, and `allocate-between-definitions`'s own control confirmed to trip only that rule — the two controls are isolated from each other, checked by running the full `GAP_RULES` list against each.

Two real, out-of-scope-for-Task-4 findings surfaced by this validation, reported to the orchestrator rather than fixed (blast zone was `src/toaster/conformance.py`, `DEFERRED.md`, `tests/test_conformance.py`, `decisions/next-passes.md`, `decisions/log.md` only): (1) `tests/test_interconnection.py`'s `QUALIFIED_ALLOC_AND_INTERFACE_SOURCE` fixture's own `allocate ApplyHeat to Toaster::heating` carries the DL-058 pattern on its `Toaster::heating` end (already anticipated by next-passes.md item 23's own text, confirmed by direct probe); (2) `tests/test_query.py`'s `INHERITED_ALLOCATION` fixture (`allocation alloc allocate doApply to Toaster::heater;`, package-level, end reaching into `Toaster`'s own nested `heater`) also carries the pattern, which was NOT previously flagged anywhere — its own test, `test_allocations_for_follows_supertypes`, asserts `conformance.language_gap_findings(m) == []`, an assertion that is false once this rule exists, so the full suite has one failing test outside Task 4's blast zone. Neither fixture was edited; the new rule was not weakened to avoid either failure.
Principles applied: F2 (skip what cannot be judged rather than guess); F4 (the same "skip rather than falsely flag" posture the file's other two gap rules already take, applied to an unresolvable declaring context); P5 (the check is proved against real content and against the two other rules' controls, not merely asserted); "flag it, don't work around it" (both new findings reported as evidence, not silently fixed or used to justify weakening the rule).
Reasoning: the discriminating test is exactly DL-058's own canAccess/checkConnectorTypeFeaturing reasoning, specialized to what the API-JSON export actually exposes: a dot-chain end's `chainingFeature` resolution is the tool's own feature-chain resolution, already gated on accessibility; a single-segment end's qualified name is the only place OpenSysML accepts an inaccessible reference with no diagnostic, and its declaring context (owner vs. not, package vs. not) is exactly the featuring-context question DL-058 answered. The empirical validation (nine real fixtures, four inline test fixtures, two isolation checks) is what confirms the algorithm matches the ruling rather than merely resembling it.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — this entry records completion of already-scoped follow-up work opened by DL-058; it opens no new judgment call. The two newly-found fixture non-conformances are themselves left as open questions for the orchestrator (whether to extend a future task's blast zone to `tests/test_interconnection.py` and `tests/test_query.py`), not ruled on here.
Provenance: `decisions/log.md` DL-058 (full spec citations: KerML 1.1 Beta 2 canAccess 8.3.3.3.4 p. 188, isFeaturedWithin p. 190, validateSubsettingFeaturingTypes p. 204, 8.3.4.5.3 checkConnectorTypeFeaturing/defaultFeaturingType pp. 214-215); `decisions/next-passes.md` item 23; `DEFERRED.md` D-019, D-020, D-032; `src/toaster/conformance.py` `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility`, `GAP_RULES`; `tests/test_conformance.py` (new tests under "allocate-connector-end-accessibility (DL-058)"); `tests/test_interconnection.py` `QUALIFIED_ALLOC_AND_INTERFACE_SOURCE`; `tests/test_query.py` `INHERITED_ALLOCATION`, `test_allocations_for_follows_supertypes`.

**Addendum (allocate-fix/task5-harden-guard, 2026-09-29, after an independent review of Task 4's algorithm):** the review, verified against the OMG pilot as ground truth rather than merely re-derived, found two real bugs in `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility`, both fixed in Task 5. **F1 (false positives):** the check compared a single-segment end's declaring context against the allocation's own owner by exact identity only, ignoring that canAccess/isFeaturedWithin also treat a featuring type as accessible when it (transitively) specializes, or (for a Usage owner) is typed by, the declaring context — KerML treats FeatureTyping as a kind of Specialization. Two pilot-confirmed-accepted (exit 0) constructs were wrongly flagged: `part def BetterToaster :> Toaster { allocate doApply to heater; }` (owner specializes the declaring context) and `part toaster : Toaster { allocate doApply to heater; }` (owner is a plain Usage typed by the declaring context, no `:>` at all). Fixed by additionally accepting a declaring context in `query.supertypes_transitively(model, owner_qn)` — confirmed empirically that this ONE helper call already covers both cases with no separate "resolve the owner's own type" code path: `supertypes_transitively(model, "P::toaster")` already returns `{"P::Toaster"}` directly, because `Symbol.specializations` (which the helper's `specialization_graph` walks) already folds FeatureTyping in as a kind of specialization. **F2 (false negatives):** the check's own docstring, and this entry's Reasoning above, claimed a multi-segment (dot-chain) end is "already structurally proven accessible by the tool's own parser" — this is FALSE. OpenSysML accepts `Toaster.heater` (dot after a bare Definition name) and `Outer::box.t` (dot after a qualified path into an inaccessible Usage) as loadable multi-segment `end_path` chains, but the pilot rejects both ("Couldn't resolve reference to Feature 'Toaster'", and "Must be an accessible feature" respectively) — no gap rule caught either. Fixed by running the SAME accessibility test against the FIRST segment of every end regardless of segment count (only the segments AFTER the first are genuinely proven safe by successful loading), plus a distinct check that a chain root whose own `@type` is a Definition is never an accessible Feature at all (scoped to `len(end) > 1` only, so it does not double-flag alongside `allocate-between-definitions`, which already owns the single-segment "whole end is a Definition" case via `end[-1]`). Both fixes re-validated with zero regressions: all 9 real fixtures (`ch01`-`ch08`, `ch10`) still clean; the rule's own negative control still fires (2 findings, still isolated from the other 3 rules); `allocate-between-definitions`'s own control still doesn't trip this rule; `FLOW_SOURCE`/`ALLOC_SOURCE`/`NESTED_SOURCE` still produce zero findings from this rule. New test coverage added for the unresolvable-declaring-context skip branch, a same-context qualified end, both F1 true-negatives, and both F2 true-positives (now correctly flagged).

Also corrected in this addendum: the "D-019 and D-020 remain open tool holes with no guard" claim in this entry's Decision paragraph (and in `DEFERRED.md` D-032 and `decisions/next-passes.md` item 23) was wrong — both already have their own `GapRule` guards (`allocate-between-definitions`, `part-typed-only-by-item-def`), predating this whole remediation effort. Fixed in `DEFERRED.md` (D-019, D-020, D-032) and `decisions/next-passes.md` (item 23). And `decisions/next-passes.md` item 24 (whether a conformant rewrite of `tests/test_interconnection.py`'s `QUALIFIED_ALLOC_AND_INTERFACE_SOURCE` fixture exists without losing `test_qualified_allocation_target_reuses_the_part_node`'s dedup coverage) is refuted, not left open: promoting a named `action doApply : ApplyHeat;` to package level and nesting the allocation inside `part def Toaster` fixes both the pre-existing `allocate-between-definitions` violation and the DL-058 pattern while keeping the qualified-path target (`Toaster::heating`) that test needs, unchanged. Verified: `model.ok` True, zero findings from all four `GAP_RULES`, and both dependent tests pass with their existing assertions unchanged. Full suite: 379 passed, 0 failed (up from 373 at Task 4's tip; 6 new tests added, 1 fixture fixed). Checkpoint suite unchanged: 7 passed, 0 failed.
Provenance (addendum): independent review of `allocate-fix/task4-gap-rule` @ 6d981aa; OMG pilot probes (same invocation as DL-058's own evidence log) against `part def BetterToaster :> Toaster { ... }`, `part toaster : Toaster { ... }`, `Toaster.heater` and `Outer::box.t`-style fixtures; `src/toaster/query.py` `supertypes_transitively`, `specialization_graph`; `src/toaster/conformance.py` `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility` (hardened); `tests/test_conformance.py` (new tests); `tests/test_interconnection.py` `QUALIFIED_ALLOC_AND_INTERFACE_SOURCE` (fixed).

**Addendum 2 (allocate-fix/task6-harden-guard-2, 2026-09-29, after a SECOND independent review of Task 5's algorithm):** the review, again verified against the OMG pilot as ground truth rather than merely re-derived, found two more real bugs in `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility`, both fixed in Task 6. **F-A (false positives — accessibility still blind to an UNNAMED owner's own supertypes/typing):** Task 5's F1 fix accepted a declaring context found in `query.supertypes_transitively(model, owner_qn)`, but that helper builds its graph from `model.query(select=["name"])` — NAMED elements only. An allocation whose owner is anonymous or a redefining usage with no declared name of its own (e.g. `part redefines heater { ... }` or `part :>> heater : Heater2 { ... }`, qualified name like `P::Better::@0`) got `supertypes_transitively(model, "P::Better::@0") == set()` even though it clearly redefines/retypes an accessible declaring context. Five pilot-confirmed-accepted (exit 0) constructs were wrongly flagged: an unnamed shorthand redefinition retyped to a subtype (`part :>> heater : Heater2 { ... }`), a completely anonymous typed usage (`part : Toaster { ... }`), an unnamed redefinition with no explicit re-type at all (`part redefines heater { ... }`), an unnamed redefinition explicitly re-typed (`part redefines heater : Heater2 { ... }`), and a NAMED redefining usage (`part heater2 : Heater2 :>> heater { ... }`, included as the control that first confirmed the pattern itself is pilot-valid). Fixed by adding `query.supertypes_transitively_raw` (`src/toaster/query.py`): an ApiIndex-native BFS that walks an element's own raw API-JSON `type`, `subsets`, `redefines` and `specializes` reference fields directly (each may be a single dict ref or a list; confirmed empirically which shape each field takes) rather than going through `Symbol.specializations`, so it works identically for named and unnamed elements. Confirmed empirically that this is a strict extension, not a different algorithm: it reproduces `supertypes_transitively`'s own results exactly on every named case Task 5 already handled (a Definition specializing another, a Usage typed by a Definition, a subsetting Usage, a two/three-level specialization chain) — so it replaces that call unconditionally in this check (a single, unified code path), not as a hybrid/fallback. **F-B (double-flagging with `allocate-between-definitions` on a NESTED Definition end):** a single-segment end resolving whole to a nested Definition (`allocate doApply to Toaster::Inner;` where `part def Toaster { part def Inner; }`) was flagged by BOTH `allocate-connector-end-accessibility` and `allocate-between-definitions`, when the pilot gives exactly one error ("Couldn't resolve reference to Feature 'Toaster::Inner'"). The existing docstring and DL-059's own first addendum both claimed the two rules never double-flag — true only for a PACKAGE-level bare Definition reference (whose declaring context happens to resolve to a plain `Package`, already exempted), not a NESTED one (whose declaring context resolves to another Definition, not exempted). Fixed by checking the end's own resolved element for a single-segment end (`len(end) == 1`) BEFORE computing any declaring context at all: if it is itself a Definition, skip unconditionally, leaving it entirely to `allocate-between-definitions` — package-level or nested makes no difference. Scoped strictly to single-segment ends; a multi-segment chain root already had its own, separate Definition-check from Task 5's F2 fix, unchanged.

Re-validated the full probe suite built for this and the prior round (~30 fixtures total, all against the OMG pilot): the five F-A false positives and the F-B double-flag now match the pilot exactly (zero findings on the five, exactly one finding from `allocate-between-definitions` alone on the nested-Definition case); every previously-passing fixture (9 real chapter models, both rules' own negative controls, `FLOW_SOURCE`/`ALLOC_SOURCE`/`NESTED_SOURCE`/`QUALIFIED_ALLOC_AND_INTERFACE_SOURCE`, and every other Task 5 probe) produces the identical result as before — zero regressions. Four additional adversarial fixtures were tried to find a third bug (an unnamed owner nested two levels deep; a redefinition chain where `redefines` points at something that itself unnamed-`redefines` something else; a three-level subsetting chain; and an attempt at an owner whose own type is itself unnamed, which turned out not to be constructible as valid SysML — the closest realizable case, an unnamed-to-unnamed redefinition chain, is covered by the redefinition-chain fixture instead) — none broke the rule; all match the pilot. Full suite: 385 passed, 0 failed (up from 379 at Task 5's tip; 6 new tests added, 0 fixtures changed). Checkpoint suite unchanged: 7 passed, 0 failed.
Provenance (addendum 2): independent review of `allocate-fix/task5-harden-guard` @ 89ce542; OMG pilot probes (same invocation as DL-058's and addendum 1's own evidence) against unnamed/redefining-owner fixtures (`e1`-`e4`, `d9` in the review's probe set) and a nested-Definition-end fixture (`b4`); `src/toaster/query.py` `supertypes_transitively_raw` (new), `_raw_refs` (new); `src/toaster/conformance.py` `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility` (hardened again); `tests/test_conformance.py` (six new tests, Task 6 section).

**Addendum 3 (allocate-fix/task7-final-hardening, 2026-09-29, after a THIRD independent review of this rule — the first reviewed Task 4 (this entry's own, unlabeled addendum above), the second reviewed Task 5 (Addendum 2), this one reviewed Task 6 — narrowly scoped to exactly two named bugs, both pre-dating Task 6):** the review, again verified against the OMG pilot as ground truth, found two more real bugs, both design gaps from Task 4's original design (not introduced by Tasks 5 or 6), fixed here. **F-1 (a Definition in the MIDDLE of a multi-segment chain was invisible to every rule):** `allocate-connector-end-accessibility` only ever checked the chain's FIRST segment (is it accessible?); `allocate-between-definitions` (D-019, pre-existing, unrelated to this whole effort's earlier tasks) only ever checked the chain's LAST segment (`end[-1]`, is it a Feature not a Definition?). A MIDDLE segment resolving to a Definition was checked by neither — verified fixture: `allocation a allocate doApply to toaster.Inner.heater;` (`toaster : Toaster`, `Toaster { part def Inner { part heater : HeatingSystem; } part inner : Inner; }`) loaded `ok=True` with zero findings from all four `GAP_RULES`, while the real OMG pilot rejected it ("Couldn't resolve reference to Feature 'Inner'"). Fixed by extending `_allocate_between_definitions` (the existing D-019 rule) to check EVERY segment of `idx.end_path(end_ref)`, not just the last — this fits D-019's own KerML 8.3.3.3.9 ReferenceSubsetting constraint ("the referenced element must be a Feature") better than adding more logic to the already-complex accessibility rule: ANY segment resolving to a Definition is exactly D-019's violation, regardless of position in the chain. Since this also covers the chain-root case (position 0), the now-redundant chain-root Definition check inside `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility` (the one Task 5 added, scoped to `len(end) > 1`) was removed, unifying it with the pre-existing single-segment (F-B, Task 6) skip into one unconditional rule: if the end's own first segment resolves to a Definition, `allocate-connector-end-accessibility` skips it unconditionally (single- or multi-segment, package-level or nested alike), leaving it entirely to `allocate-between-definitions` — this avoids reopening Task 6's own double-flag risk on the chain-root case specifically. **F-2 (the Package exemption missed LibraryPackage):** `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility`'s declaring-context exemption used exact-match `context_element.get("@type") == "Package"`, missing `LibraryPackage` (a `library package`'s own distinct `@type` in the API-JSON export) — verified fixtures: `library package L { part def HeatingSystem; part heat : HeatingSystem; } ... allocation a allocate doApply to L::heat;` (direct qualified reference) and the more realistic `private import L::*; ... allocation a allocate doApply to heat;` (import then bare reference) both loaded `ok=True` with the pilot accepting both (exit 0), while the rule wrongly flagged the first (and would flag the second too, depending on how the import resolves the declaring context — confirmed empirically it does). Fixed by changing the exemption to `context_element.get("@type", "").endswith("Package")`, matching this file's own established convention for metaclass-family checks (`_is_or_specializes_part_definition`'s precedent).

Re-validated the full probe suite built across this and the prior three rounds (53 fixtures total: 33 in `probes/`, 20 in `rev6/`, all re-run against all four `GAP_RULES` and the real OMG pilot). Before/after diff across both directories: exactly 8 fixtures changed, all consistent with the two fixes and none else — `c3_defchain`, `d7_both_ends_bad`, `d8_nested_def_chain_root` and `r19_unnamed_redef_chain_root_def` moved their existing finding from `allocate-connector-end-accessibility` to `allocate-between-definitions` with the SAME total count (the chain-root-Definition reassignment); `r05_middle_def_chain` and `r14_four_seg_def_middle` gained their first finding (0 -> 1, from `allocate-between-definitions`, F-1's own fix); `r17_library_package_end` and `r20_toplevel_library_import` lost their wrong finding (1 -> 0, F-2's own fix). All 45 other fixtures in both directories: identical rule and finding count to before this task (two fixtures, `b4_nested_def_end` and `r13`, keep the same rule and count but their `allocate-between-definitions` message text changed, from the old end-index-based wording to the new per-segment wording introduced by this task's loop rewrite — a cosmetic difference from the segment-iteration change, not a behavior change). `d8_nested_def_chain_root.sysml` (chain-root-is-Definition case) still produces exactly ONE finding total, now from `allocate-between-definitions` instead of `allocate-connector-end-accessibility`, pilot-confirmed rejected. `b4_nested_def_end.sysml` (single-segment Definition end, Task 6's own F-B fixture) is confirmed completely unaffected: still exactly one finding, from `allocate-between-definitions`, unchanged in both source and count, since it never touches the chain-root branch this task removed. r05/r14 pilot-confirmed rejected (exit 1, matching the new finding); r17/r20 pilot-confirmed accepted (exit 0, matching the now-empty finding list). All 9 real chapter fixtures (ch01-ch08, ch10; no ch09): still zero findings from all four `GAP_RULES`. Both rules' own negative controls re-confirmed isolated (`test_gap_rule_negative_controls_are_isolated`): `allocate-between-definitions`'s own control (`allocate ApplyHeat to HeatingSystem;`, two single-segment Definition ends) is single-segment on both ends, so the segment-iteration extension does not change its result or trip the other rule; `allocate-connector-end-accessibility`'s own control (`allocate ControlSystem::applyHeat to Toaster::control;`, both ends Features) is unaffected by either fix, since neither end's first segment is a Definition and neither declaring context is a (Library)Package. The full re-validation sweep surfaced five additional pilot/`GAP_RULES` disagreements (`b5_second_seg`, `d1_private_second`, `d2_protected_second`, `f4_qualified_ix`, `r15_def_middle_inside_owner`) that made this reviewer stop and check before calling "stop here" correct — investigated individually: `f4_qualified_ix` is a harness artifact of this task's own re-validation script (it hardcodes the pilot's root-element argument as `"P"`; `f4`'s own package is named `ToasterDemo`, so the pilot's failure is "can't find root element 'P'", unrelated to any of the four `GAP_RULES` entirely), not a real disagreement at all. The other four (`b5`, `d1`, `d2`, `r15`) all have `model.ok == False` in OpenSysML itself (`unresolved member: Inner` for `r15`'s structurally-different nesting; a genuine missing-member reference for `b5`; and OpenSysML's own visibility enforcement rejecting a `private`/`protected` member access from outside its declaring context for `d1`/`d2`) — all four are caught by the tool's own load-time conformance, outside the "language gap findings" domain by design (`language_gap_findings` only inspects `ok=True` models), and all four are BYTE-IDENTICAL in `GAP_RULES` behavior before and after this task's fix, confirming they are pre-existing, out-of-scope, and not a fifth bug category opened by this task. Full suite: 388 passed, 0 failed (up from 385 at Task 6's tip; 3 new tests added — F-1's `r05`-equivalent, F-2's qualified and import forms — 0 fixtures changed, one existing test's assertion updated to reflect the corrected rule ownership on the chain-root case). Checkpoint suite unchanged: 7 passed, 0 failed.

After four independent review rounds, each verified against the OMG pilot as ground truth and each finding real bugs, and this task fixing the last two named ones, further open-ended hardening of this rule pair is deliberately NOT being pursued. This is treated as a well-validated (against 53 real and synthetic probe fixtures across four rounds, plus 9 real chapter fixtures, all confirmed against the real OMG pilot as ground truth) best-effort scoping check for an always-on tutorial guard rule, not a claim of formal completeness — a real, deliberate scope decision worth recording, not silence. The reviewer's own suggestion to promote the probe fixture set into a permanent, checked-in differential test (so a future round does not have to rebuild this validation by hand) is recorded as `decisions/next-passes.md` item 25, not implemented here.
Provenance (addendum 3): independent review of `allocate-fix/task6-harden-guard-2` @ 84b9972; OMG pilot probes (same invocation as the prior three rounds' own evidence) against a middle-segment-Definition chain (matches `probes/rev6/r05_middle_def_chain.sysml`, `r14_four_seg_def_middle.sysml`) and library-package fixtures (matches `probes/rev6/r17_library_package_end.sysml`, `r20_toplevel_library_import.sysml`); full re-run of `probes/*.sysml` and `rev6/r*.sysml` (53 fixtures) against all four `GAP_RULES` and the pilot; `src/toaster/conformance.py` `_allocate_between_definitions` (extended to every segment), `_allocate_connector_end_accessibility` (chain-root check removed, Package exemption widened); `tests/test_conformance.py` (three new tests, `dot_chain_inaccessible_root_flagged`'s existing assertion updated); `DEFERRED.md` D-019, D-032; `decisions/next-passes.md` item 25 (new).

## DL-060 | 2026-09-29 | EFFICIENCY-BINDING-FIX | `efficiency`'s bare-literal binding was the one other real pilot warning in the whole model; fixed across ch07/ch08/ch10; a genuine, separate OpenSysML unit-display bug the fix surfaced is documented (D-033), not worked around in the model

Path: Handled by orchestrator (mechanical conformance fix + a bounded judgment call on how to treat the display side effect it surfaced, neither reopening a Standing Assumption nor needing Z's or the ACE's read)
Decision: following DL-058's remediation, ran the real OMG pilot against every real chapter fixture (ch01-ch08, ch10) with both errors and warnings captured, not just errors. Found exactly one remaining finding: `attribute :>> efficiency = 0.7;` (`DimensionOneValue`, a structured `ScalarQuantityValue`) bound with a bare, unit-less literal, warned "Bound features should have conforming types" — silently tolerated by OpenSysML and sysml-toolkit, caught only by the pilot, the same class of gap DL-058 found. Fixed to `attribute :>> efficiency = 0.7 [MeasurementReferences::one];`, using the library's own dimensionless-unit reference (confirmed `SI::one` resolves in neither OpenSysML nor the pilot's own library, so `MeasurementReferences::one` is the correct, not a workaround, reference). Confirmed clean (no error, no warning) on all three pinned tools, on all three affected fixtures. Every other model-conformance-relevant `DEFERRED.md` entry (D-014, D-021, D-026, D-027) was checked against real content and confirmed already correctly categorized (staged project checks with no KerML constraint behind them, an already-worked-around tool evaluation bug, or a pattern never actually used) — nothing else to revert.

Fixing the binding surfaced a real, separate finding: `deliveredEnergy`'s result now prints an unsimplified compound unit (`MeasurementReferences::one*SI::'kg⋅m²⋅s⁻²'`) instead of the clean `SI::J`, though the underlying value is unchanged (`model.eval(...) == 67200.0 [SI::J]` still holds — confirmed a label-only defect, not an arithmetic one: `Unit.factors` and `scale_num` are identical before and after the binding fix). Two model-side alternatives would avoid the ugly display (retyping `efficiency` as a bare `Real`, or stripping the unit inside the `deliveredEnergy` calc body with `.num`) — reviewed and rejected: both trade real modeling rigor (a properly-typed, unit-bearing quantity) for a cosmetic OpenSysML display bug, which cuts against this project's own established practice (D-003 and others) of documenting a genuine tool gap rather than distorting the model to dodge it. Recorded as `DEFERRED.md` D-033 instead; the two affected notebook cells and `chapters/ch07-execution/index.md`'s Expected Result now state the real printed output alongside the physically meaningful unit.
Principles applied: the same DL-058 "prove-then-use" discipline (empirically verify against the real pilot, not trust the other two tools' silence); F4 (evidence comes from analysis, not from what happens to render cleanly); the gap-tracking rule (a confirmed tool gap gets a `DEFERRED.md` entry, never a silent model distortion).
Reasoning: `efficiency`'s binding was genuinely non-conformant in the same sense DL-058's `allocate` pattern was — a construct two of three pinned tools silently tolerate but the pilot (closest to a real reference implementation) rejects. The display-quirk finding is different in kind: the model is now MORE correct, and the remaining oddity lives entirely in how a evaluator prints its own result, not in what the model says. Choosing to document rather than paper over that in the model matches how every other confirmed tool gap this project has found has been handled.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — applies the existing gap-tracking rule and DL-058's own verification discipline to a new, independently-found instance; does not reopen any Standing Assumption.
Provenance: DL-058, DL-059 (the verification discipline this follows); `DEFERRED.md` D-003 (the pre-existing, confirmed-correct workaround this builds on), D-033 (new); `MeasurementReferences.sysml`'s own `attribute one : DimensionOneUnit[1]` declaration; `models/ch07-cumulative.sysml`, `ch08-cumulative.sysml`, `ch10-cumulative.sysml`; `chapters/ch07-execution/01-calc-energy.ipynb`, `index.md`; `tests/test_predecessor_containment.py`.

## DL-061 | 2026-09-29 | EXERCISE-RIGOR-DECISION | The exercise track will mirror the main chapters' full layer discipline, not stay a simpler parallel design; settles `next-passes.md` item 9's own open question, escalated directly to Z

Path: Escalated to Z
Decision: Z decides the re-derived exercise track (`exercises/ch01` through `ch10`) will mirror the main chapters' now-current layer discipline — the same conventions just re-established across Pass 4 and the DL-058/efficiency remediations (the conformant nested `allocate` idiom, properly unit-typed quantities, judgment records where the corresponding main chapter has them, the construct-and-analyze loop) — rather than staying a deliberately simpler, lighter-weight parallel design. `next-passes.md` item 9 (found during PASS4-002) had explicitly left this choice open, naming both readings and the concrete drift it was already causing (exercises depending on pre-DL-018 default-value patterns several main chapters no longer use; Ch6's exercise still asking for the exact "completeness" overclaim Ch6's own contract spent six review rounds removing, item 15; Ch7's exercise still hand-copying a sympy relation and missing a real state machine, item 16).
Principles applied: pedagogical consistency (an exercise that teaches a pattern the main chapter just spent real review effort removing actively undermines that chapter's own lesson, not merely a stale detail); the same "never silently defer real non-conformance" posture this session applied to DL-058 and the efficiency binding, extended here to pedagogical/layer non-conformance rather than KerML conformance.
Reasoning: exercises exist to let a learner practice the technique the chapter just taught; an exercise built on a superseded pattern teaches the wrong habit at exactly the moment of most direct practice. The main chapters' current conventions are themselves the product of substantial, hard-won review effort (Pass 4's audits, DL-030's mechanism/interface separation, DL-058's allocate conformance, DL-060's quantity typing); holding exercises to a lower bar would mean the tutorial's own practice problems contradict its own worked examples.
Determined: yes.
Extension: yes — resolves `next-passes.md` item 9's own explicitly-left-open design question; not a mechanical application of an existing rule.
Provenance: `decisions/next-passes.md` items 9, 15, 16 (the open question and its two concrete instances); Pass 4's own chapter audits; DL-058, DL-060 (the layer/conformance rigor this decision extends to the exercise track).

## DL-062 | 2026-09-29 | EXERCISE-SCOPING-SURVEY | Exercises ch09/ch10 will extend the coffee-maker domain (not revert to the toaster's own bread-handling model); scopes the exercise-track re-derivation before work begins

Path: Escalated to Z
Decision: a full read-through survey (all 10 `exercises/chNN/exercise.ipynb`, their corresponding main-chapter `index.md`/`conclusion.md`, and `models/chNN-cumulative.sysml`) found: ch01 needs no changes (already Pass-4-rebuilt, matching DL-061's now-decided rigor); ch02/ch03/ch04/ch08 need small-to-medium mechanical fixes (unit-typing bare literals, an `item def Brew`/`action def Brew` name collision, a stale-fixture pointer at `tests/fixtures/probe.sysml`'s pre-rigor overclaim); ch05/ch06/ch07 need substantial, specific construct-level fixes exactly matching this session's own recent work — ch05's `allocate Brew to BrewUnit;` is the same unqualified definition-to-definition idiom DL-058 replaced in the main chapters; ch06 asks for the exact "claim the decomposition is complete" overclaim DL-030-era review spent six rounds removing from main ch06 (next-passes.md item 15); ch07 hand-copies a sympy relation and has a state machine missing a `do action` and completion transitions, exactly next-passes.md item 16's finding. ch09 and ch10 are pre-Pass4, pre-coffee-maker-convention leftovers: 2-cell notebooks pointing at `models/ch09-snapshot.sysml`/`ch10-snapshot.sysml`, neither of which exists anywhere in the repo, asking about the toaster's own "bread-handling" requirements/allocations — breaking the coffee-maker-domain convention every other exercise (ch01-ch08) deliberately uses so a learner applies the construct rather than copies the toaster's own solution.

Z decides ch09/ch10 will extend the coffee-maker domain: a coverage table and traceability graph built over the learner's OWN completed ch08 coffee-maker exercise model (which, per the survey, already has a real requirements/verification story — `BrewReq`, `verify_satisfaction()`, an `asserted_solution` record, `check_stale()` — sufficient substrate to extend), mirroring main ch09/ch10's own pattern (query the immediately-prior chapter's model directly, no new model element), rather than reverting to the toaster's bread-handling model. This restores domain consistency across the whole exercise track and gives genuine coverage/traceability practice against a model the learner built themselves.
Principles applied: pedagogical consistency (the same reasoning DL-061 already applied — an exercise that breaks the track's own established convention right at its capstone undermines the practice value of everything built up to that point); parallel-domain-by-design (the coffee-maker domain's whole purpose, stated by the exercises' own convention, is forcing genuine application rather than transcription; reverting to the toaster's model at the very end would let a learner who struggled with ch01-08 simply re-read the toaster's own answer instead).
Reasoning: the survey confirmed the coffee-maker domain already carries enough of a requirements/verification substrate (built across ch06-ch08's own exercises) to support a coverage/traceability capstone without inventing new content from nothing; the dead `snapshot.sysml` references and the domain break both look like leftover artifacts from before the coffee-maker convention existed, not a deliberate design choice, so restoring consistency is the more defensible reading of what these two chapters were meant to be.
Determined: yes.
Extension: yes — resolves a second design question the survey raised, not covered by DL-061's own text, which only settled the rigor-level question, not the domain question for ch09/ch10 specifically.
Provenance: this survey (dispatched by the orchestrator after DL-061); `exercises/ch09/exercise.ipynb`, `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`; `chapters/ch09-coverage-sufficiency/index.md`, `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/index.md` (main ch09/ch10's own reuse-the-prior-fixture precedent); DL-061.

## DL-063 | 2026-09-29 | EXERCISE-CH05 | ch05's exercise carried a real D-019 non-conformance (allocate-between-definitions) AND substituted an unrelated construct for the one main ch05 actually teaches (port def / conjugated port / named interface); fixing it surfaced a third "two tools tolerate, pilot rejects" gap (`filter` is a reserved SysML v2 word), now D-034

Path: Handled by orchestrator (mechanical/design fix within the already-authorized exercise-track re-derivation; the `filter`-rename call was a bounded engineering judgment, not a Standing Assumption or design-fork question)
Decision: `exercises/ch05/exercise.ipynb`'s step 1 (`allocate Brew to BrewUnit;`) allocated between two DEFINITIONS (`action def Brew`, `part def BrewUnit`), tripping the pre-existing `allocate-between-definitions` GapRule (D-019) — confirmed via `conformance.language_gap_findings()`, a different non-conformance than DL-058's own accessibility pattern (both ends here are Definitions, not qualified paths into a nested Usage). Fixed by nesting a usage-to-usage allocation inside `CoffeeMaker`'s reopened body (`allocation brewAllocation allocate brew.applyWater to brewUnit;`), mirroring `heatAllocation`'s real idiom exactly, using the inherited `perform action brew` feature (ch01) and `applyWater`'s own nested step (ch04's own earlier fix). Separately, step 2 substituted a plain item `flow` for a construct main ch05 never uses at all — main ch05 notebook 03 teaches `port def`, a conjugated port (`~DurationPort`), and a NAMED `interface ... connect ...;`, none of which the exercise exercised. Fixed with a coffee-domain mirror (`WaterPort`/`WaterPump`/`FilterBasket`/`CoffeeFlow` with a named `waterInterface`), confirmed clean (`model.ok`, `language_gap_findings == []`, `build_interconnection_intent` correctly extracting the flow) before dispatch.

Fixing bug 2 surfaced a new, real gap: the confirmed-clean design used `part filter : FilterBasket;`, and `filter` turned out to be a reserved word in the pilot's own SysML v2 grammar (used for view-filter conditions) — OpenSysML and sysml-toolkit both accept it as an ordinary bare identifier with zero diagnostic; the pilot rejects it (exit 1). This is the THIRD instance of the DL-058/DL-060 "two of three pinned tools tolerate, pilot rejects" pattern, in a third distinct corner of the grammar (connector-end accessibility, quantity-unit binding, now a reserved-word collision). The builder tried escaping the reserved word (`'filter'`) — the pilot accepts this, but `build_interconnection_intent`'s own rendering then carries the literal quote characters into flow-endpoint strings (`"'filter'.waterIn"`), an artifact nothing else in this tutorial produces. Renaming the part to `filterUnit` (a purely cosmetic identifier change, no functional difference) avoids the artifact entirely and was confirmed clean across all three tools — the better fix, not a compromise. Recorded as `DEFERRED.md` D-034 (no `GapRule` guard built: unlike D-019/D-020/D-032, this is a narrow lexical collision against a fixed, small reserved-word set, not a structural/semantic non-conformance pattern, so a full guard was judged disproportionate).

Also fixed, found by the exercise's own mandatory pointer sweep (next-passes.md item 26): main ch05 notebook 01's own pointer promised a `model.find()` returning `None` for a nonexistent element, which the exercise didn't do (added); decided, per item 26's "list analysis operations too" lesson, to add a `port_type_mismatches()` check step mirroring main ch05 notebook 03's own analysis (confirmed empty on the real solution). A real, pre-existing (not caused by this fix) cross-chapter name collision was found and reported, not fixed: `exercises/ch06/exercise.ipynb`'s own step 2 also declares `FilterBasket`, colliding with this chapter's own `FilterBasket` if a learner's cumulative model carries both — folded into next-passes.md for ch06's own future contract.
