Decision: `exercises/ch09/exercise.ipynb` was a dead, pre-Pass4 2-cell stub pointing at a nonexistent `models/ch09-snapshot.sysml`, asking about the toaster's own pre-coffee-domain "bread-handling requirements." Per DL-062's already-made decision (ch09/ch10 extend the coffee-maker domain rather than reverting to the toaster's model), rebuilt as a genuine, one-for-one mirror of real ch09 (`chapters/ch09-coverage-sufficiency/01-requirement-coverage.ipynb`, `02-evidence-completeness.ipynb`, `03-stale-detection.ipynb`) over the learner's own accumulated ch01-ch08 coffee model: a polarity-aware requirement-coverage join (`brewReq` covered both ways via `rated`/`weak`, `tempCheck` one-sided via `hot`'s negative claim only, mirroring `heatGenerationReq`/`timely`'s exact real-chapter shape, confirmed empirically not assumed), a Hawkins sufficiency reading applied to the learner's own `AS-C06-EX`/`AS-C08-EX` judgment records (the inductive-vs-deductive premises argument, a placeholder-record negative example), and a stale-detection-at-scale pass over both records together.

Three necessary adaptations from the main chapter's own pattern, since exercises never commit a solution fixture (no answer key exists to point at): (1) the model-load cell is a blank placeholder (the learner pastes their own full model) rather than `Path(...).read_text()` against a committed file; (2) `AS-C06-EX` is hashed against a SECOND placeholder, `ch06_source` (the learner's own ch06-stage model, mirroring how `exercises/ch06/exercise.ipynb` itself already hashes the record), not against the current ch08-stage model, so the real asymmetric staleness result (one record already stale from real ch06→ch07 growth, the other still current) is genuinely reproduced rather than substituted with a same-hash symmetric result; (3) `AS-C06-EX`/`AS-C08-EX` are reconstructed as scaffolds (fixed structural fields real, prose fields left as the same hint strings the source exercises use), not finished text.

Two independent review rounds, each catching a real, substantive gap:
- **Round 1 FAIL**: (F1) the first draft hashed BOTH records against the same shared model source, claiming this was a "necessary adaptation" — false; `exercises/ch06/exercise.ipynb`'s own real record already hashes against a ch06-stage variable every learner has, so the single-hash design was a quiet re-baseline, not a forced constraint. Fixed by adding the second `ch06_source` placeholder and a `difflib` growth-diff cell mirroring real notebook 03's own, reproducing the true asymmetric result. (F2) the sufficiency-reading step omitted the premises/`assumption_refs` argument real notebook 02 makes central to Hawkins' own idea (sufficiency is specifically about whether stated premises make a conclusion follow) and gave the learner nowhere to record a judgment. Fixed by adding the premises-printing cell, the inductive-vs-deductive argument, and a `# Your sufficiency reading here` placeholder. Five non-blocking findings (a stale field-fixed/hint summary, an over-strict edit string, an over-asserted coverage claim the source ch06 exercise doesn't actually pin down, three stale pointer-attribution texts, a grammar error) fixed in the same round; one (the pointer-text fix) needed a direct orchestrator commit since the builder correctly flagged its own contract had put those files outside its declared blast zone while also asking it to fix them.
- **Round 2 PASS**, with one finding (N1) fixed directly before merge: the new `ch06_source` guard cell only asserted the string changed, which a mutation test (leaving the placeholder unfilled) proved passes vacuously — the SAME class of gap DL-067 spent three rounds catching in ch08. Fixed with a real guard (load the model, confirm it resolves `WaterMover`, confirm it genuinely predates `transferEfficiency`), confirmed to correctly raise on an unfilled placeholder and to still pass on a real solution. Two cosmetic nits (a hint list omitting `criteria`, an ambiguous pronoun) fixed in the same pass.

Also recorded: `DEFERRED.md` D-035 (sysml-toolkit warns rather than errors on a `satisfy` naming an undeclared requirement — the reverse-direction case of the D-032/D-033/D-034 three-way-tool-disagreement pattern; found and confirmed during this contract, orchestrator added the entry since it was outside the builder's own blast zone).
Principles applied: the same DL-058/DL-060/DL-061/DL-063/DL-065/DL-066/DL-067 "prove-then-use, mirror the real construct, don't invent a substitute" discipline; DL-067's specific "a demonstration a mutation test can falsify is not yet a real demonstration" standard, now caught proactively in review rather than needing a third round.
Reasoning: choosing the two-placeholder split (F1) over simply disclaiming the single-hash design's limitation was the same judgment this whole track has made repeatedly — when a stronger, fully faithful mirror is directly achievable with a small addition, prefer it over a weaker, honestly-caveated substitute.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — applies the existing exercise-track rigor and gap-tracking rules to new instances; reopens no Standing Assumption.
Provenance: `decisions/log.md` DL-058, DL-060 through DL-067 (the discipline and infrastructure this extends); `decisions/next-passes.md` (item 9's original survey); `exercises/ch09/exercise.ipynb`; `chapters/ch09-coverage-sufficiency/01-requirement-coverage.ipynb`, `02-evidence-completeness.ipynb`, `03-stale-detection.ipynb`, `conclusion.md`; `DEFERRED.md` D-035.

## DL-069 | 2026-09-30 | EXERCISE-CH10 | ch10's exercise was a dead pre-Pass4 stub; ground-up rewrite as a traceability graph, judgment ledger and sign-off synthesis over the learner's own coffee model — the last chapter of the full exercise-track re-derivation

Path: Handled by orchestrator (builder dispatch + two independent review rounds within the already-authorized exercise-track re-derivation)
Decision: `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb` was a dead, pre-Pass4 2-cell stub pointing at a nonexistent `models/ch10-snapshot.sysml`, asking about the toaster's own "bread-handling allocation links." Per DL-062's already-made decision, rebuilt as a genuine mirror of real ch10 (`chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb`, `02-judgment-synthesis.ipynb`, `03-engineering-signoff.ipynb`): a traceability graph tracing `brewReq` (subject `wm : WaterMover`, allocated via `BrewAssembly::moverAllocation`, realized by `Impeller`, bidirectional evidence `rated`/`weak`) and `tempCheck` (subject `bu : BrewUnit`, no further physical subtype, allocated via `CoffeeMaker::brewAllocation`, one-sided evidence via `hot` only) through subject/allocation/realization/verification-evidence, plus the inverse "unjustified widget" search confirming `deliveredMassBoundedBySupply` (ch08's Z3-proved lemma) ties to no requirement; a judgment ledger reconstructing `AS-C06-EX`, `AS-C08-EX`, `AI-C06-EX` verbatim from the merged ch06/ch08 exercises; and a new synthesis record `AI-C10-EX` (`asserted_inference`, premises = the ledger plus the graph's own findings) honestly scoped to `engineering_conclusion="undetermined"`.

One genuine, confirmed query-shape divergence from the toaster, not forced to match: `specializes_transitively("CoffeeDemo::BrewUnit")` also returns `BrewAssembly` (since `BrewAssembly :> BrewUnit`, unlike the toaster where nothing specializes `Toaster`) — fixed with a `PartUsage`-type filter, documented as a real finding.

Two independent review rounds:
- **Round 1 FAIL** on three findings: (F1) both commits carried co-author trailers — rebuilt with identical trees, trailers stripped, verified byte-identical diffs against the originals. (F2) the reverse "unjustified widget" search — checking whether the lemma's own id ever appears as a `SatisfyRequirementUsage`'s `subsets` target — is inherited verbatim from the real chapter and is genuinely broken there too: it can NEVER return `True` for any loadable model (a `satisfy` target must itself be a requirement usage, so a bare constraint's id can never structurally appear there — the check reports something guaranteed true by construction, not a discovery) and separately misses a real tie (a `requirement def` whose own constraint subsets the lemma directly creates a genuine tie the check still fails to detect, confirmed by construction). Decided: do not redesign the query (that's the main chapter's own future fix, recorded as `next-passes.md` item 29) — narrow every claim in the exercise to describe exactly what the check verifies, cite item 29, state the gap plainly. (F3) the `ch06_source_pre_impeller` guard only checked for `WaterMover`, already present in ch06 Step 1 (before `BrewAssembly`/`brewReq` exist), so pasting the wrong, too-early stage passed silently — fixed with real assertions on `BrewAssembly`/`brewReq` presence and a query-based (not substring) `Impeller`-absence check, mutation-tested to confirm it now correctly rejects the previously-silent case.
- **Round 2 FAIL** on one finding: two cells (a `AS-C08-EX` intro paragraph, and four of `AI-C10-EX`'s own hint strings) still made the unqualified overclaim F1's fix elsewhere had already narrowed, making the notebook internally contradict itself. Fixed directly by the orchestrator, not sent back to a third builder round (a small, purely textual consistency fix, not a new design question) — reworded both to match the already-established narrowed framing, and hardened one more analogous substring test (`ch06_source_final`'s `transferEfficiency` check) to a query, for the same reason F3 hardened its sibling. One cell-type bug introduced during this direct fix (a `NotebookEdit` call targeting the wrong cell id, silently converting the `conn.close()` code cell into markdown text and overwriting it with prose) was caught immediately by re-running the full cell sequence before committing, not left for review to find. This last-mile fix was verified directly by the orchestrator rather than sent through a third independent review round: full end-to-end re-execution via `nbclient` against a freshly-built, independent solution model, a mutation test confirming the hardened guard now correctly rejects the wrong model stage, and the full test suite — no new SysML construct or tool-facing surface was introduced (pure Python assertion logic and markdown prose), so no new sysml-toolkit/pilot conformance check applied beyond what round 2 already confirmed clean.

Also recorded: `decisions/next-passes.md` item 29 (the real chapter's own vacuous/missed-tie search bug, confirmed by the round-1 reviewer's own constructed counter-example, tracked as its own future contract, not fixed here — a main-chapter content bug, not an exercise-mirroring issue).

**This completes the full exercise-track re-derivation** (DL-062 through DL-069): all ten exercises (`ch01` through `ch10`) now mirror their real chapters' full layer discipline, construct-by-construct, each independently reviewed against all three pinned tools (OpenSysML, sysml-toolkit, and the restored OMG pilot, DL-064), with every genuine gap found along the way recorded (D-034, D-035, next-passes.md items 26-29) rather than silently patched over.
Principles applied: the same DL-058/DL-060 through DL-068 "prove-then-use, mirror the real construct, don't invent a substitute" discipline, holding to the very end of the track; the specific standard DL-067/DL-068 established (a demonstration a mutation test can falsify is not yet a real demonstration) applied one final time to F3's own guard.
Reasoning: narrowing the exercise's own prose rather than redesigning the query (F2) keeps this contract's blast radius to what it was actually scoped for — a real bug found in already-reviewed main-chapter content deserves its own dedicated contract and review cycle, not a rider on an exercise fix.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — applies the existing exercise-track rigor and gap-tracking rules to new instances; reopens no Standing Assumption.
Provenance: `decisions/log.md` DL-058, DL-060 through DL-068 (the discipline and infrastructure this extends and completes); `decisions/next-passes.md` items 9, 26, 29; `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`; `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb`, `02-judgment-synthesis.ipynb`, `03-engineering-signoff.ipynb`, `conclusion.md`.

## DL-070 | 2026-09-30 | CH10-WIDGET-SEARCH | The real chapter's own "unjustified widget in reverse" search (`decisions/next-passes.md` item 29) is fixed: a general `requirement_ties`/`tied_to_any_requirement` helper replaces the structurally-vacuous, false-negative-prone `SatisfyRequirementUsage.subsets`-only check

Path: Handled by orchestrator (dedicated builder dispatch, own worktree, own review cycle, per DL-069's own note that this is a main-chapter content bug, not an exercise-mirroring issue)
Decision: `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb` (cell `a7413515`) and `03-engineering-signoff.ipynb` (cell `50c87f41`) both asked whether `deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply` (Chapter 8's Z3-proved conservation lemma) ties back to any requirement, by testing `delivered_energy_bounded["@id"] in satisfy_targets`, where `satisfy_targets` was built from `SatisfyRequirementUsage.subsets` alone. Confirmed broken two ways, both directly against the pinned tools: (1) SysML v2's own `assert satisfy` grammar requires a satisfy's `subsets` target to itself be a requirement usage (formal/2026-03-02 SS8.3), so a bare `AssertConstraintUsage`'s own id can never appear there for ANY model that loads, in OpenSysML, sysml-toolkit or the OMG pilot alike -- the old check reported something guaranteed true by construction, not an empirical finding. (2) A genuine tie can exist a different way: a `requirement def`'s own `require constraint c :> deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;` puts `subsets` directly on `c`, an element owned by the requirement but never a `SatisfyRequirementUsage` at all -- the old check still reported `False` against a model containing exactly this construct. Fixed by adding `requirement_ties`/`tied_to_any_requirement` to `src/toaster/query.py`: for every element in the model (via `ApiIndex`), check its own `subsets`, `redefines` and `references` fields (a list confirmed empirically by probing the real API-JSON export for each construct, not borrowed from `supertypes_transitively_raw`'s own `_RAW_SUPERTYPE_FIELDS`, which is `("type", "subsets", "redefines", "specializes")` -- an overlapping but different list, since it also walks `type`/`specializes` and never walks `references`) for a match against the target's id, then walk the raw `owner` chain upward, inclusive of the tying element itself, to find the nearest ancestor whose own `@type` is `RequirementDefinition` or `RequirementUsage`. Both main-chapter notebooks and the exercise's own coffee-domain mirror (`exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`, narrowed by DL-069 to describe the old check's own limitation) now use the fixed, general helper; the exercise's own prose no longer says "narrow check" since the underlying bug is fixed. The real model's own finding is UNCHANGED (`deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply` is still tied to no requirement) -- confirmed by re-executing both main-chapter notebooks end to end via `nbclient` against the real, committed `models/ch10-cumulative.sysml`, with `requirement_ties(...) == []` and `tied_to_any_requirement(...) is False` -- but now for a real reason found by this broader search, not a structural impossibility dressed up as one. A round-2 fix-and-reverify (same branch, after an independent review) found this search was itself still narrower than its own first draft's "exhaustive" language claimed: a bare `require target;`/`assume target;` (a requirement reference to an already-existing constraint, not declaring a new one) or a body expression that just names the constraint directly exports as a `FeatureReferenceExpression` whose own `referent` field holds the target's id -- a different shape from `subsets`/`redefines`/`references`, which the search did not look at. `_TIE_FIELDS` in `src/toaster/query.py` now also checks `referent`, confirmed by a new positive control (`require deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;`) and re-confirmed clean in all three pinned tools; every notebook and log claim of "exhaustive" was reworded to state plainly what the search covers (every DIRECT way a tie can be made: `subsets`, `redefines`, `references`, `referent`) and what it still does not (a transitive chain through an intermediate constraint that is itself not owned by any requirement) -- see `decisions/next-passes.md` item 29's own updated resolution note.
Principles applied: the same DL-067/DL-068 "a demonstration a mutation test can falsify is not yet a real demonstration" standard, applied to a bug found in already-reviewed, already-merged main-chapter content rather than a newly-built exercise.
Reasoning: a positive control (an inline SysML fixture, not committed to any model, with `requirement def EnergyReq { subject t : Toaster; require constraint c :> deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply; }`) confirms `requirement_ties` finds the tie (`{"tying_element": "TieFixture::EnergyReq::c", "field": "subsets", "requirement": "TieFixture::EnergyReq"}`) and `tied_to_any_requirement` reports `True`; a second positive control confirms the `references` field (a `ref altName references deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;` inside a requirement) and a `RequirementUsage` (not just a `RequirementDefinition`) as the owning requirement are both covered too; a third fixture confirms a tie owned by an unrelated, non-requirement part is found but reported with `requirement=None`, so `tied_to_any_requirement` correctly stays `False` -- the search is general, not hardcoded to `deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply`'s own name. A mutation test on the first positive-control fixture confirms the OLD, narrow `SatisfyRequirementUsage.subsets`-only logic still reports `False` against it, proving the fix is not itself vacuous. The negative control (the real, current `models/ch10-cumulative.sysml`) confirms `requirement_ties(...) == []`, a real absence found by the broader search, not a narrow one dressed up as broad. Round-2 fix-and-reverify added a further positive control confirming the `referent` field (a bare `require deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;`) is now covered too, plus two more confirming the `redefines` field and a `RequirementUsage` (not a nested constraint) as the tying element itself; the negative control above was re-run unchanged and still passes with the extended field list, and `requirement_ties` now raises `KeyError` rather than returning an empty result for a target that does not resolve to any real element, so a future rename of the lemma would be caught rather than silently misreported as "no tie." Even after this fix, the search remains honestly scoped, not literally exhaustive: it does not chase a transitive chain through an intermediate constraint that is itself not owned by any requirement (e.g. `constraint mid :> deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;` outside any requirement, with a requirement's own constraint then subsetting `mid`) -- a named scope limit, not a hidden one. All three pinned tools re-verified clean on the new, general positive-control constructs (both rounds): OpenSysML v0.9.0 (`model.ok == True`, used directly by all controls above); sysml-toolkit v0.9.1 (`sysmlv2 check`, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning, 0 errors); the OMG pilot (0.62.0, `SysMLInteractive.process()`, `hasErrors=False`). Test coverage: `tests/test_query.py` gained a `ch10` fixture and, across both rounds, one test per relationship shape/owner shape demonstrated (negative control, positive controls for `subsets`/`references`-plus-usage-owner/`redefines`/`referent`/usage-as-tying-element, the not-requirement-owned case, and the mutation test) -- all pass, full suite grown accordingly, 0 failed. [Corrected 2026-09-30, DL-071: this entry originally hardcoded "eight tests" here, which was already stale by the time round 3 found this whole search still incomplete (see DL-071) -- restated qualitatively rather than re-hardcoded, and any reader wanting a live count should run `uv run pytest -k requirement_ties --collect-only -q` rather than trust a number typed into this log.]
Determined: yes.
Extension: no -- applies `supertypes_transitively_raw`'s own already-established raw-reference-field-walk pattern (DL-039/allocate-fix task6) to a new target (`RequirementDefinition`/`RequirementUsage` ownership instead of type/specialization), and the exercise track's own "prove-then-use" discipline to a main-chapter bug; reopens no Standing Assumption.
Provenance: `decisions/next-passes.md` item 29 (now marked RESOLVED, this entry); `decisions/log.md` DL-069 (the finding, not fixed there by design), DL-067/DL-068 (the mutation-test standard this reuses); SysML v2 formal/2026-03-02 SS8.3 (the `assert satisfy` grammar constraint that makes the old check structurally vacuous); `src/toaster/query.py` (`requirement_ties`, `tied_to_any_requirement`, `_nearest_requirement_owner`); `tests/test_query.py`; `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb`, `03-engineering-signoff.ipynb`; `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`.

## DL-071 | 2026-09-30 | CH10-WIDGET-SEARCH | `requirement_ties` narrowed to two small, explicitly-named checks (satisfy-by-subject; a direct reference from within a requirement's own body), after three rounds of broadening an open-ended search each found a real gap and the third raised a genuine, unresolved scope question that Z resolved by choosing to stop broadening

Path: Handled by orchestrator (dedicated builder dispatch, own worktree, own review cycle -- a fourth round on the same branch, `fix/ch10-widget-search`; this entry is corrected in place rather than superseded again, since the branch remains unmerged)
Decision (history, briefly, so the final design's shape is legible): the original check was structurally vacuous (a bare `SatisfyRequirementUsage.subsets` test can never be `True` for any model that loads, per SysML v2's own `assert satisfy` grammar). Round 1 fixed that with a small, fixed field list (`subsets`/`redefines`/`references`); round 2 added `referent` after an independent review found a bare `require`/`assume` reference missed. A third independent review found the round-2 search STILL missed real ties -- most seriously, the chapter's own idiom, `assert satisfy R by C;` (a tie on `SatisfyRequirementUsage.subject`, a field neither round ever looked at), plus `dependency`/`allocate`/`bind`/`metadata`/an invocation expression, each via yet another field name, and `Concern`/`Viewpoint` definitions/usages as genuine metaclass subtypes the owner-walk's exact string match missed. That round redesigned the search as a field-agnostic scan (every field of every element, excluding a containment/bookkeeping exclusion list) plus a metaclass-subtype-aware owner walk. A FOURTH independent review then found the field-agnostic redesign ITSELF still missed a real construct -- a connection-end (`end e1 ::> lemma;`), because its only carrier (a `ReferenceSubsetting` relationship object) happened to sit inside the very exclusion list the redesign relied on to avoid duplicate hits -- and, separately, raised a genuine, unresolved SCOPE QUESTION with no clean answer from the existing design: should a `dependency`/`allocate`/`metadata about` relationship naming both the lemma and a requirement as two SIBLING elements, neither owning the other, count as a "tie," when the whole design so far only counted references FROM WITHIN a requirement's own body? This question was escalated to Z, who decided: stop chasing an open-ended, general-purpose completeness search -- which had now been broadened three times, each broadening finding a real gap, with no sign of convergence -- and replace it with a SMALL, EXPLICITLY-NAMED, NARROWLY-SCOPED pair of checks instead, described honestly as exactly that, with no claim of generality or completeness.

The FINAL design, implemented this round: **Check A -- satisfy-by-subject.** Is the lemma ever named as the SUBJECT of a real `assert satisfy <requirement> by <lemma>;` relationship (a `SatisfyRequirementUsage` whose own `subject` field is the lemma's id)? Built directly on the existing, already-tested `satisfy_relationships`/`get_satisfy_relationships` helpers, not on new low-level element scanning: `requirement` is whatever that satisfy relationship's own `subsets` names (SysML v2's own grammar guarantees this is a real requirement). This is round 2's most important missed finding, now fixed for real, and for the first time attributed to the actual named requirement rather than requiring the `SatisfyRequirementUsage` to count as its own owner. **Check B -- direct reference from within a requirement's own body.** Does any element owned (at any nesting depth, via the ORIGINAL, never-found-buggy owner-chain walk) by a `RequirementDefinition`/`RequirementUsage` (EXACT `@type` match only -- deliberately NOT `ConcernDefinition`/`ConcernUsage`/`ViewpointDefinition`/`ViewpointUsage`/`SatisfyRequirementUsage`, even though all five are genuine metaclass subtypes) have its own `subsets`/`redefines`/`references`/`referent` field (the SAME small, fixed field list round 2 established) pointing at the lemma's id? `tied_to_any_requirement` is `True` if either check finds something owned by a requirement.

Round 3's entire field-agnostic scan machinery is DELETED, not kept alongside the narrower design: `_dict_refs`, `_STRUCTURAL_FIELDS`, `_LINK_BOOKKEEPING_TYPES`, `_is_requirement_owner_type`, `_REQUIREMENT_METACLASS_PARENTS`, and the closure-based owner-subtype walk are all gone from `src/toaster/query.py`. The goal is less code, not more: a small, auditable surface a reader can verify by inspection, not by trusting an exhaustive-but-unverifiable exclusion list. Net line-count change for `requirement_ties`/`tied_to_any_requirement` and their helpers (the comment block through the end of `tied_to_any_requirement`): 218 lines before this round (round 3's field-agnostic design) to 119 after (a 99-line reduction; `git diff --stat` for this round's edit to `src/toaster/query.py`: 81 insertions, 180 deletions).

**Explicitly, plainly OUT OF SCOPE by design, not by oversight** (each was found by an earlier round's broader search and deliberately dropped when the design was narrowed, per Z's own decision): connection-ends (`end e1 ::> lemma;` / `::=` port/connector typing); a `dependency`/`allocate`/`metadata about`/`connection`/`#derivation`-style relationship naming the lemma and a requirement as two sibling elements where neither owns the other (the scope question this round's escalation raised, now answered: no, this does not count as a tie for this design); `Concern`/`Viewpoint` ownership; a transitive chain through an intermediate element not itself owned by a requirement; and any other relationship shape not named above.
Principles applied: AGENTS.md's "implement, do not decide" -- the scope question was escalated rather than resolved by the builder; the same DL-067/DL-068 "a demonstration a mutation test can falsify is not yet a real demonstration" standard, now applied in reverse (a negative test proving a scope boundary really is as narrow as documented, not accidentally broader or narrower); reuse over reinvention (Check A reuses `satisfy_relationships`, not a new low-level scan).
Reasoning: the full, targeted test suite (`tests/test_query.py`) covers both checks and every named scope boundary. Detected (positive controls, all passing): satisfy-by-subject, the round 2's headline missed case, now correctly attributed to the real, named requirement rather than `requirement=None` (`test_requirement_ties_satisfy_by_subject_the_chapter_own_idiom`); the round-1/round-2 direct-reference shapes (`subsets`, `redefines`, `references`, `referent`, a `RequirementUsage` as the tying element itself, a multi-value `subsets` list, a chain of two requirements, a `ConcernUsage` nested inside a real requirement still correctly attributed to the enclosing requirement). Confirmed NOT detected, matching the documented scope exactly (explicit negative tests, named `..._by_design`, proving the narrow scope is genuinely narrow, not accidentally broader or narrower): a sibling `dependency`, `allocate`, `bind`, `metadata about`, an invocation expression, a `Concern`/`Viewpoint` DEFINITION as the tie's direct owner (found by Check B's field scan but correctly attributed `requirement=None`), a transitive chain through an unowned intermediate, and a connection-end (`end e1 ::> lemma;`, a NEW fixture this round, confirmed undetected for the documented reason: its only carrier is a `ReferenceSubsetting` object's own `referencedFeature`, not a top-level `subsets`/`redefines`/`references`/`referent` field on the end feature itself). The real, current `models/ch10-cumulative.sysml` still reports `requirement_ties(...) == []`/`tied_to_any_requirement(...) is False`, confirmed directly against the two narrow checks, and both main-chapter notebooks were corrected to say so plainly: this is true under the narrow checks' own stated scope, not a claim about every conceivable way a tie could exist. Both main-chapter notebooks were re-executed end to end via `nbclient` against the real model, zero errors. The exercise's own coffee-domain mirror was smoke-tested with a real `assert satisfy waterDeliveryReq by deliveredMassBoundedBySupply;`-style fixture, confirming Check A detects it there too, via the exact same, unmodified library helper. All 19 kept/built fixtures re-verified on OpenSysML v0.9.0 (clean) and sysml-toolkit v0.9.1 (`sysmlv2 check --lib`, 18/19 clean, 1 pre-existing unrelated warning on the base model); the OMG pilot (`jupyter-sysml-kernel-0.62.0-SNAPSHOT`, driven live via `jupyter_client` against a kernel launched with `ISYSML_LIBRARY_PATH` set, 18/19 clean). **New finding, unrelated to this fix, flagged not silently worked around:** the one fixture that fails on the other two tools is the connection-end negative control itself -- a bare, named `connector c2 { end e1 ::> lemma; end e2 ::> t; }` declaration loads cleanly under OpenSysML v0.9.0 but is rejected with a parse error by BOTH sysml-toolkit v0.9.1 (`expected ';' or '{', found 'c2'`, reproduced independently with multiple minimal fixtures having nothing to do with this fix, including a bare top-level `connector c2 from a1 to b1;`) and the OMG pilot (`no viable alternative at input 'connector'`), even though `spec-refs/KerML.xtext`'s own `Connector` rule (`'connector' (FeatureDeclaration? ValuePart? | ConnectorDeclaration) TypeBody`) appears to permit exactly this shape. This looks like a genuine implementation gap in both tools (or a possible over-permissiveness in OpenSysML), not something this fix introduced or can fix; it is reported here for the ACE/orchestrator to judge whether a `DEFERRED.md` entry is warranted (outside this round's blast zone). Full suite: `uv run pytest -q` reports 410 passed, 7 deselected (`-m checkpoint`, all 7 separately confirmed passing) -- 1 MORE than the pre-round-4 baseline of 409, not fewer as expected going in: this round converts 7 round-3 tests into 7 negative tests (1:1, not a reduction) and adds exactly 1 new test (the connection-end negative control, a genuinely new scope boundary this round is the first to test); no test asserting the deleted exclusion-list machinery's own class count was ever found to exist, so there was no such test to remove. `test_connect.py::test_session_opens_and_closes` and the `test_diagram_study_fixtures.py` mutated-fixture tests were re-run individually and passed cleanly (no flake observed this round).
Determined: yes.
Extension: no -- a narrowing, the opposite of an extension: less code and a smaller, more honestly-bounded claim than round 3's own design; reopens no Standing Assumption.
Provenance: this entry's own three predecessors, all on the same branch (`fix/ch10-widget-search`): the original DL-070 fix and its round-2 fix-and-reverify (both folded into DL-070's own text), and this entry's own prior round-3 text (the field-agnostic redesign, now itself superseded and corrected in place here); the round-4 reviewer's own connection-end finding and sibling-relationship scope question, escalated to Z via the orchestrator; Z's own decision to narrow rather than keep broadening; `src/toaster/query.py` (`requirement_ties`, `tied_to_any_requirement`, `_nearest_requirement_owner`, `_REQUIREMENT_OWNER_TYPES`, `_TIE_FIELDS`); `tests/test_query.py`; `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb`, `03-engineering-signoff.ipynb`; `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`; `decisions/next-passes.md` item 29.

**Addendum (2026-09-30, round 5, a fifth independent review, PASS with one blocking wording finding and non-blocking findings fixed directly by the orchestrator, no further review round needed):** the `_TIE_FIELDS` comment still said "Every DIRECT reference-field shape SysML v2 uses," directly contradicting the surrounding "SMALL, FIXED, NAMED field list, not an attempt at an exhaustive scan" sentence two lines later -- fixed to "The four reference fields this check reads." Two further findings, both decided and fixed directly: (1) Check A counted a NEGATED `assert not satisfy R by lemma;` as a tie, which the reviewer correctly flagged as undocumented and semantically backwards -- a negative claim asserts the lemma does NOT meet the requirement, reinforcing "untied," not establishing a connection; fixed to exclude `is_negated` claims, mirroring the polarity distinction every other coverage-style check in this module already makes (`requirement_coverage`'s own `satisfied_by`/`failed_by` split), with a new test (`test_requirement_ties_negated_satisfy_does_not_count_as_a_tie`). (2) The connection-end negative-control fixture used `connector c2 { ... }`, a KerML-only keyword not valid in a `.sysml` file at all (confirmed: even a NESTED `connector {}` is rejected by sysml-toolkit and the pilot, not only a top-level one) -- so the test's "not detected" result held for a confounded reason (invalid syntax), not cleanly for the intended one; fixed by switching to the tool-confirmed-valid `connection c2 { end e1 ::> lemma; end e2 ::> t; }` form, re-verified clean on all three pinned tools. `DEFERRED.md` D-036 (added by the orchestrator, outside this branch, on `pass1/harness-alignment` directly) was corrected to match this sharper framing -- not "a bare TOP-LEVEL connector," but "the `connector` keyword itself is KerML-only and should not validly appear in a `.sysml` file," which better fits the confirmed evidence and leaves it genuinely open which tool(s) are non-conformant relative to the grammar. Full suite after this addendum: 411 passed (410 + the new negated-satisfy test), both main-chapter notebooks re-executed end to end with zero errors.

## DL-051 | 2026-09-27 | PASS4-000 | SA-8 relaxed for structural (layer-separation) increments; Z's decision, escalated directly, not an ACE ruling

Path: Escalated to Z
Decision: Z relaxes SA-8 ("one new construct or analysis operation per sub-notebook") for a structural increment specifically: a notebook whose job is separating what one existing element conflates across the functional/logical/physical layers (DL-030's fix — moving `efficiency` to a bounded logical carrier and adding a separate energy-balance inequality is the motivating case) may introduce the small set of constructs one layer boundary genuinely requires together (for example `perform action`, an `abstract part def`, and a named `allocate`, introduced as one coherent idea), rather than being forced to spread them across more notebooks or being blocked by SA-8's letter. SA-8's spirit (no combining unrelated constructs for unrelated reasons) is unchanged; the exception is scoped to layer-separation, not general convenience. Options considered and not chosen: redrawing notebook boundaries to keep SA-8 exactly as written (more, smaller notebooks per chapter); an unscoped case-by-case exception (rejected as too open-ended to log or apply consistently).
Principles applied: P6 (reopening SA-1 to SA-9 is Z's alone; the ACE and the orchestrator do not rule on it, only surface it); F6 is preserved (the boundary tests and per-layer idiom are unchanged, only the notebook-granularity packaging is relaxed).
Reasoning: Pass 4's re-derivation is required by DL-030 to properly separate a mechanism, its interface, and its carrier from the functional layer — that separation is one idea with several necessary parts, not several unrelated ideas; forcing it across artificially split notebooks would fragment one coherent boundary-test explanation into pieces that don't stand alone, which works against the same didactic clarity SA-8 exists to protect. The relaxation is scoped narrowly (structural/layer-separation increments only) so it does not license combining unrelated constructs for convenience elsewhere.
Determined: yes.
Extension: yes — SA-8 is a binding Standing Assumption; this is Z reopening and narrowing it, not the ACE applying it to a new case.
Provenance: AGENTS.md (SA-1 to SA-9 binding); ace-protocol "Never re-open SA-1 through SA-9 without A8 logging and routing to Z"; decisions/next-passes.md §6 (the SA-3/SA-8 collision, flagged); DL-030 (the motivating fix); decisions/pass4-backlog.md §2.

## DL-072 | 2026-09-30 | ENERGY-TIE-RECONCILE | Two independently-built designs for tying Chapter 8's Z3-proved conservation lemma to a requirement are reconciled into one final design: Approach B's subject-less `EnergyConservationReq`, minus the `assert satisfy` line Approach B's own design kept, dropped by direct test, not argument alone

Path: Handled by orchestrator (dedicated builder dispatch, own worktree, own review cycle; the full working is recorded in `docs/case-studies/2026-09-30-energy-conservation-requirement-tie.md`, not duplicated here)
Decision: Two same-day attempts at tying `deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply` (Chapter 8's Z3-proved conservation lemma, found tied to no requirement by DL-070/DL-071's own search) to a stated requirement were reconciled. Approach A (`requirement def EnergyConservationReq` with a declared `subject heatGen : HeatGenerator`, a `verification def`, placed in Chapter 8; built, reviewed twice, and merged under its own work contract ENERGY-TIE-08, reverted clean at commit 85b7778) was found, on later inspection, to violate SysML v2 formal/2026-03-02 §7.21.1 in spirit: its own declared subject was never referenced by its own required constraint at all, a real subject-type concern found by direct spec reading, not by any tool diagnostic — Approach A never added an `assert satisfy` line, so nothing in it ever exercised that subject for the pilot to check. The pilot's own real "Bound features should have conforming types" warning fired against a different, separately-built draft instead: Approach B's own earliest commit (`26e184e`) paired a typed subject with a real `assert satisfy` line, which Approach B's own author fixed by dropping the subject two commits later (`56100bb`), before this reconciliation began. Approach B (`fix/ch10-tie-remediation`, an independently-built, unmerged branch, same day) fixed the subject issue (no declared subject, inheriting `RequirementCheck`'s own default, `Anything`), placed the fix in Chapter 10 (where DL-070/DL-071's own search finds the gap), and added a new judgment record, `AC-C10`. But Approach B also kept an `assert satisfy energyConservationReq by deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;` line (Check A), on the reasoning that it gives this chapter's own traceability search a second, independent hit. Direct, empirical testing (not argument alone — the case study's own central finding) showed that line does not merely add nothing: `model.verify_satisfaction()` errors identically regardless of what is bound as the satisfying feature (`require condition evaluation failed: no value for feature heatGenCheck.efficiency`), because `EnergyConservationReq`'s own required constraint never references its subject at all — a structural fact confirmed against the base library's own `RequirementCheck.result` definition, not inferred. Separately confirmed this round: `sysmlv2 verify --solve` (a different tool, the CLI solver) generates no check at all for the bare subsetting reference once `assert satisfy` is removed — neither `undecided` nor any other verdict — so no solver-level evidence of any kind exists for this requirement beyond the lemma's own already-cited proof (AS-C08). The final design, now implemented: Approach B's subject-less `EnergyConservationReq`/`energyConservationReq`, tied to the lemma by `require constraint c :> deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply;` (subsetting, from within the requirement's own body — SS7.21.2's own sanctioned reuse idiom) alone, with the `assert satisfy` line dropped entirely and a doc comment (plus a real negative-control demonstration in notebook 01) stating plainly why. `requirement_ties()` now reports exactly one entry for the lemma (Check B's own hit, attributed to the DEFINITION, `EnergyConservationReq`, not the usage); `requirement_coverage()` reports `energyConservationReq` as `covered=False` (`satisfied_by=[]`, `failed_by=[]`) by design, a structurally different kind of "uncovered" from `timely`'s own real gap, now explicitly distinguished in Chapter 9's own prose (`01-requirement-coverage.ipynb`, `index.md`) so a reader does not misread it as a second, undiscovered instance of the same failure mode; `satisfy_relationships()`'s own count stays at 4, unchanged, since a bare subsetting reference is not itself a satisfy-kind element. `AC-C10` (notebook 01) is revised to drop every claim that depended on Check A (the `requirement_coverage()`-covered=True citation, the "satisfied by the lemma" framing) while keeping its own core, honest finding intact: a requirement identified from existing evidence, after the fact, to close the very gap that evidence's absence created, is a real, named assurance deficit about circularity — not resolved by this round, named rather than hidden, the same as Approach B's author originally intended. `AI-C10` (notebook 03) and the exercise's own coffee-domain mirror (`exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`, which had neither approach yet) are updated to match.
Principles applied: AGENTS.md §1.6 ("judgment is never eliminated... an evidence base and explicit justification"); the same DL-067/DL-068/DL-070/DL-071 "a demonstration a mutation test (or, here, a direct tool run) can falsify is not yet a real demonstration" standard, applied to a design argument rather than a code fix: the semantic-vacuity argument against Check A was correct, but was only confirmed, and sharpened past what the argument alone said, by actually running `model.verify_satisfaction()` against it. "Writing to the checker versus writing to be true" (the case study's own sharpest framing): Check A existed, in Approach B's own design, for reasons closest to "give `tied_to_any_requirement` a second way to find it" — not because the construct's own spec semantics called for it, and not because it was needed (Check B already succeeds alone) — exactly the trap this tutorial's own "unjustified widget" pedagogy is about, in reverse.
Reasoning: two independently built candidate designs, neither aware of the other, each caught something the other's own review process missed (Approach B's own author hit a subject-type mismatch as a live pilot diagnostic, in B's own first draft, before B's own fix dropped the subject; Approach A's own two independent review cycles reasoned about its own declared-but-unused subject abstractly and did not identify it as a defect in its own right) — real, accidental redundancy doing real work, not planned. The trade study (spec faithfulness, didactic clarity, tool usage, in Z's own stated priority) favored B outright on the first two axes and ran even on the third. The deeper interrogation of Check A specifically (six distinct angles, recorded in full in the case study: the letter of SS7.21.1, the spec's own expressed intent for what a satisfy's binding is for, tool support as evidence of legality not of purpose, the base library's own open-template counterexample `massLimit` versus `deliveredEnergyBoundedBySupply`'s own closed, already-proved form, what this model's own intent actually is, and the "good enough already reached one step earlier" reading of SS7.21.1's own satisfaction criterion) converged on dropping it before the empirical test ran at all — the test did not just confirm the argument, it found something the argument alone understated (the construct does not merely add no information; it errors when exercised the way its own siblings are legitimately exercised elsewhere in this model). Verified directly against the real, corrected model, not assumed to generalize from the isolated probe that informed this decision: `requirement_ties()` returns exactly one entry; `requirement_coverage()` lists three named requirements with the stated `covered` values; `satisfy_relationships()` stays at 4; `sysmlv2 verify --solve` emits no line at all for the new requirement once `assert satisfy` is gone (confirmed by contrast against a one-off model with it added back in, which does emit an `undecided` line — the mechanism Approach B's own `AC-C10` evidence had relied on, and which does not survive Check A's removal). Full test suite and `scripts/check_construction.py --check` both re-run clean after the change (see this contract's own builder report for the pasted output).
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — resolves a design question two independently-built candidates left open (which representation of the tie is correct), using the trade-study and escalation machinery `ace-protocol`/`orchestrator-protocol` already provide; reopens no Standing Assumption. Reverting Approach A's own merged commit (85b7778, prior to this entry) was a mechanical cleanup of an already-identified defect, not a new design decision in itself.
Provenance: `docs/case-studies/2026-09-30-energy-conservation-requirement-tie.md` (the full working: the trade study, the six-angle interrogation of Check A, the empirical confirmation, the steelman considered and set aside — this entry points to it rather than restating it); `decisions/log.md` DL-070, DL-071 (the search this tie closes the gap for); `decisions/next-passes.md` item 29 (closed by this entry); SysML v2 formal/2026-03-02 §7.20.1–7.21.4; `models/ch10-cumulative.sysml`; `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb`, `02-judgment-synthesis.ipynb`, `03-engineering-signoff.ipynb`, `index.md`, `conclusion.md`; `chapters/ch09-coverage-sufficiency/01-requirement-coverage.ipynb`, `index.md`; `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb`; `tests/test_query.py`.

## DL-073 | 2026-10-01 | USER-TESTING-SKILL-FIX | COMPLETE: `user-testing` skill fixed for two process-level bugs the Pass 3 battery itself surfaced but which the skill (unchanged since) never absorbed

Path: Handled by ACE (Z directed, in chat, after reviewing the Pass 3 battery's drift: "fix the user-testing skill's known issues first", ahead of a planned fresh battery run against current `main`)
Decision: Two concrete, already-diagnosed process bugs in `.claude/skills/user-testing/SKILL.md`, confirmed still present (`git diff bcc4796 origin/main -- .claude/skills/user-testing/` is empty — the skill has not changed since the Pass 3 battery that found these): (1) the "Execution checklist" and "Report format" sections name cells by a fixed numeric index ("Cell 0", "Cell 5", "Cell 6", ...), which assumes every chapter notebook has exactly the 7-cell minimum skeleton; this already contradicts `toaster-recipe`'s own established rule ("Additional markdown+code pairs may be inserted... A6 reviews for skeleton completeness by content type, not by cell index") and does not accommodate a construction-zone notebook's multiple fragment cells, a judgment-record notebook's 17-27 real cells, or Chapter 10's own distributed-seam design (seam carried by prose across several query cells, not one dedicated cell) — all real shapes already in the real tutorial. (2) The skill gives the Novice persona (Haiku 4.5) no rule for what "exactly one sentence" means beyond the words themselves, and the Pass 3 battery recorded (per the project's own memory of that battery, decisions/log.md DL-053 through DL-062 on `pass3/user-testing`) that persona repeatedly misjudging a real, single, semicolon-joined sentence as "two sentences" across four different chapters (Ch1, Ch7, Ch8, Ch9) — every instance checked out as a genuine single sentence, a persona-calibration false positive, not a content defect. Intended change: (1) rewrite the "Execution checklist"'s 9 steps and the "Report format"'s "STRUCTURAL CHECKS" block to identify each item by what it does ("the concept-statement cell", "the model-increment cell(s)", "the seam cell(s)", etc.) rather than by fixed index, with one prefatory sentence establishing the content-type rule and citing `toaster-recipe`'s own precedent; (2) add one parenthetical to the concept-statement check giving a concrete, checkable rule for "one sentence" (count terminal periods, not semicolons or conjunctions). Neither change touches the persona table, the model assignments, the ACE synthesis protocol, the pass bar, or what counts as blocking/not-blocking — those are unaffected by this fix and not touched.
Blast-radius check (`skill-editor` Step 2): does not affect more than one archetype's primary skill (user-testing is `simulated-learner`/`ace`-only); does not remove a prohibition; does not add a capability beyond the original design (it corrects how an existing checklist locates existing content); does not touch `sysml-v2-toaster-model`'s construct list; does not affect a learning outcome (this changes how the TESTER locates chapter content, not what a learner sees). No escalation triggered.
Revert record: `git show 27c3f35:.claude/skills/user-testing/SKILL.md` (the file's full content immediately before this edit, 101 lines).
Determined: yes.
Extension: no — corrects an ambiguous, already-incorrect assumption in a builder-facing testing skill (ACE-fixable per `skill-editor`'s own list: "clarification of an ambiguous rule that a developer demonstrably misread" and "tighter prohibition derived from an observed error pattern"); touches no Standing Assumption, no learner-facing content, and no persona/model assignment.
What changed: the "Execution checklist" (9 steps) and both parts of the "Report format" (`STRUCTURAL CHECKS` and `EXECUTION RESULTS`) now identify cells by content type ("the concept-statement cell", "the model-increment cell(s)", "the seam cell(s)", etc.) rather than fixed numeric index, with one prefatory sentence explaining why (construction-zone and judgment-record notebooks have more cells; Chapter 10 carries the seam across several cells' prose rather than one dedicated cell) and citing `toaster-recipe`'s own, already-established "by content type, not cell index" rule as precedent. The concept-statement check gained one parenthetical giving a concrete rule for "one sentence" (count terminal periods, not semicolons or conjunctions) to close the Novice persona's own recorded false-positive pattern. Nothing else in the file changed: personas, model assignments, the ACE synthesis protocol, and both "what counts as blocking" lists are untouched.
Post-edit check: re-read the modified sections and both adjacent sections (`Personas and model assignment` above, `ACE synthesis protocol` below) — no adjacent rule weakened or contradicted. `uv run python -m glossary check` — ok, 0 errors, same 7 pre-existing warnings. Found, and deliberately did not fix (different file, out of scope for this entry, would need its own DL pre-edit gate): `.claude/skills/tutorial-style-guide/SKILL.md` lines 70-72 carry the identical fixed-cell-index assumption ("Cell 0", "Cell 5", "Cell 6") independently — flagged for whoever next touches that skill, not fixed here.
Provenance: `decisions/next-passes.md` (the Pass 3 battery's own now-superseded findings this entry draws the two process-level bugs from, per this session's own direct review of how far `pass3/user-testing` has drifted from current `main`); `.claude/skills/toaster-recipe/SKILL.md` ("by content type, not cell index", already-established precedent this fix now applies consistently in `user-testing` too); `.claude/skills/skill-editor/SKILL.md` (the gate this entry follows).

Brief (Z's idiom):
DECISION NEEDED: In a judgment-record notebook (no SysML construction of its own; its one construct is a Hawkins ReviewRecord), what are the three things AGENTS.md 1.10's seam must connect?
Objective: a learner who was never told there are three worlds still sees that authored formal text, the tool that runs it, and the printed result are distinct and connected -- as behavior, not prescribed text (1.10). Design space, typed by what counts as "model text":
  A. Record triad counts -- the notebook's introduced construct is the record (F4, DL-033), so record fields / validate_record / [] is a genuine instance of the seam; the model side must still be shown loaded somewhere in the notebook (it is).   B. SysML triad only -- 1.10 names "model text, the tool that loads it, the rendered result"; judgment notebooks must also close with one sentence tying printed cumulative source / load-or-eval tool / printed model-side result; the record triad is additional. Consequence: one builder contract, one sentence per judgment notebook; recipe unchanged.
  C. One bridged seam -- the record already binds to the model in code (`model_ref`, `content_hash = hash_content(source)` of the printed text); the seam sentence narrates that bridge, making the two triads one connection and incidentally fixing the silent-load minor. Consequence: one sentence per judgment notebook, no lens vocabulary.
Feasibility: all three fit SA-7/SA-8 and 1.10's never-name clause. Utility: A keeps the model/analysis split visible (F4); B makes judgment notebooks read like siblings; C does both at one sentence. MoE: can every persona point at the three things (all can today under A; the Practitioner reading of 1.10 says no under B). Judgment: this is a reading of 1.10, not derivable from it; residual uncertainty is whether Z intends "model text" literally (SysML) or as "the formal text this notebook introduces".
Recurrence: fourth independent flag (pass3 Ch2 Returning Learner; pass3 Ch3 Novice and Returning Learner; fresh Ch2 all three personas; fresh Ch3 SE Practitioner, fb7e26b) -- and more occurrences from this same fresh battery landed after this entry was drafted; route all of them to this entry rather than re-escalating.

## DL-084 | 2026-10-01 | HAWKINS-ANCHOR | Judgment records anchored to the model (subject_ref + ReviewRecordRef); closes DL-075

Path: Implemented a Z-directed design (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-hawkins-judgment-record-anchor-design.md) and its implementation plan (docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-hawkins-judgment-record-anchor-plan.md), both approved in conversation; executed overnight via the orchestrator/builder/reviewer pipeline (builder = sonnet, reviewer = opus, every task independently reviewed on a different model than its builder, per decisions/work-contract-template.md).
Decision: `ReviewRecord` gains `subject_ref: str` (src/toaster/evidence.py), the tutorial's own narrowed analog of Hawkins' Assurance Claim Point -- a single, checkable qualified name a judgment is directly about, anchored on both the Python side (`subject_ref`, checked by `validate_record`'s new required-ness/resolution/cross-representation rules) and the model side (a new `metadata def ReviewRecordRef { attribute identifier : String; }` construct, SysML v2 formal/2026-03-02 SS7.27.2, introduced once in Chapter 2 and carried forward in every later cumulative model). `src/toaster/query.py` gains `get_review_record_refs()`, the model-to-Python direction. Every original-authoring judgment record across Ch2/3/4/6/8/10 (AC-001, AC-C03, AS-C03, AI-C04, AC-C06, AS-C06, AI-C06, AS-C08, AC-C10) now carries a real `subject_ref` and a matching model tag; AI-C10 (Ch10's own synthesis) stays exempt under the rule's own escape hatch (`asserted_inference` with non-empty `premises`), confirmed by actually running `validate_record` against the real model, not by assertion alone. Every reconstruction/ledger citation (Ch9, Ch10's own ledger) carries the value forward in Python only. Every pre-existing negative control that would otherwise have gained an unintended second validation error under the new required-ness rule (AI-BAD x2, the empty-identifier "broken" record x2, AS-BAD, AS-PLACEHOLDER, and one negative control in `exercises/ch09` the plan's own AST-based inventory had missed entirely) was fixed to keep demonstrating exactly its own one intended failure. `exercises/` -- out of either the spec's or the original plan's scope, added as Task 15 only after Task 1's own reviewer found the gap -- was retrofitted the same way, including correcting a deeper error Task 15's own builder caught and flagged rather than silently improvising past: every exercise notebook runs a parallel `CoffeeDemo` exercise, not an extension of `ToasterDemo`, so the plan's literal ToasterDemo-qualified values were wrong-domain; every value was swapped to its real CoffeeDemo analog (read from each record's own pre-existing `model_ref` field), and one reviewer-caught definition-vs-usage error in that swap (`AC-C10-EX`) was itself caught and fixed in a second review pass. `toaster-review-protocol` and `toaster-recipe` are updated: the former documents `subject_ref` as the tutorial's ACP analog and corrects its own construction-zone diagram, which (discovered only during Task 11's own review) had never been updated to show the new anchor-fragment group or the `model=` argument to `validate_record`, three sources (the diagram, the shipped notebooks, and toaster-recipe's own prose) having silently drifted from each other; the latter's seam-cell guidance now states that a judgment-record notebook's seam narrates one bridged connection (tagged SysML text, the cross-checking tool, an agreement result) rather than a choice between the model's own construct/tool/result triad and the record's own fields/validate_record/result triad. The glossary's existing, previously-uncited `assurance-claim-point` term (`glid:def-hawkins--assurance-claim-point`) gains a `gl:refines` edge from the tutorial's own convention, correctly entered as `gl:status gl:proposed` with no `gl:confirmedBy` (an agent proposes; only Z confirms -- `tutorial-glossary` SKILL.md rule 1), in `glossary/definitions/tutorial.ttl` under `glid:def-tutorial--assurance-claim-point`, not the file/id a first draft used before its own review corrected it.
**This closes DL-075.** The recurring judgment-record seam escalation (ten occurrences across the fresh user-testing battery, DL-075 through DL-083, Z's decision left pending throughout) asked which three things AGENTS.md 1.10's seam must connect in a notebook whose only new content is a judgment record: the record's own fields/validate_record/[] (reading A), the SysML text/tool/result triad (reading B), or a bridge between them (reading C, the escalation's own recommended default, pointing at `model_ref`/`content_hash=hash_content(source)` as the existing-but-unnarrated bridge). This design makes reading C concrete and literally checkable rather than a judgment call between readings: every judgment-record notebook's seam cell now narrates the tagged SysML text (`subject_ref`'s own `ReviewRecordRef` usage, printed), the tool that loads and cross-checks both representations (`validate_record(record, model=model)` plus `get_review_record_refs(model)`), and a result the reader has just watched -- a printed `Model tag: {...}` line whose `annotated_element` the reader can see agrees with `subject_ref` -- immediately before `Validation errors: []`. This is a stronger, checkable instance of reading C, not a selection among A/B/C by argument; DL-075's own escalation text is retired as moot, not resolved by a Z ruling among its three options.
Principles applied: F4 (a judgment record is analysis of the model, not the model itself -- the anchor makes that analysis checkable against the model without blurring the boundary); P4 and heuristic 6 (every narration change earned its place by being independently re-verified, not merely asserted); P5 (prefer a result the reader has watched over one merely claimed -- the whole design, and the specific fix to the seam cell that initially omitted the Model-tag print, Task 7); P6 (every judgment call a builder encountered beyond its own contract's literal text was reported, not silently resolved -- the ApplyHeat case-mismatch, Task 5; the TOASTER_INCREMENT restructuring, Task 9; the CoffeeDemo-domain error, Task 15); the repo's own review discipline (decisions/work-contract-template.md: author and reviewer never the same model) -- exercised in full, catching real defects in 6 of 15 tasks on first review (3, 6, 7, 9, 10, 12, 15), each fixed and re-verified before integration.
Reasoning: The design was grounded in a direct read of the primary source (Hawkins et al. 2011, the authors' own self-archived copy, not the glossary's prior curated extracts alone) before any code was written, confirming the paper's own domain-generality claim (SS5, "the concepts apply immediately to any property of interest") licenses this tutorial's use of the framework, and locating the actual gap: an Assurance Claim Point is never free-floating, every confidence argument anchors to one located assertion, and `ReviewRecord` had no equivalent -- `model_ref`/`content_hash` bind to a whole file, never to a specific element. The model-side half of the anchor (a real SysML metadata construct, not just a validated Python string) was proven feasible by a live probe against OpenSysML v0.9.0 before being written into the design, following this repo's own prove-then-use discipline, and re-probed independently by this session's own Task 2 builder before implementation, with exact JSON shapes (list-valued `type`/`annotatedElement`, the `ownedMember`-to-`LiteralString` chain for a tag's own `identifier` attribute) confirmed empirically rather than assumed from the spec's own earlier probe. Execution followed the plan's own declared dependency order (Tasks 1-2 parallel; 3 through 9 strictly serial, since every one shares the same cumulative-model files; 10-12 parallel once Task 3 landed; 15 after its chapter dependencies; 13-14 last), integrated by cherry-pick rather than branch merge throughout, because every agent worktree in this session was independently found to be provisioned from a stale base commit missing the plan's own recent history -- a real, repeated environment quirk, not a content defect, worked around the same way every time once diagnosed (Task 2's own reviewer first caught and named it).
Determined: yes, for every task's implementation and every reviewer-found defect's fix, each independently re-verified (full test suite, `scripts/check_construction.py --check`, and end-to-end notebook execution via nbconvert, not assumed from a builder's own report) before integration. Not determined, left as recorded gaps below rather than resolved unilaterally: a narrow, real asymmetry in `validate_record`'s own cross-representation check (an exempt `asserted_inference` record that is given `subject_ref=""` but DOES have a matching model tag is never cross-checked, since the check is gated on `subject_ref` being non-empty -- flagged by Task 5's reviewer; affects no record that exists in this tutorial today, since the one exempt record, AI-C10, correctly carries no tag at all).
Extension: yes, narrow. F4 extended to cover a judgment record's own anchor: a `subject_ref`/`ReviewRecordRef` pair is itself model-side metadata, not a model element the record is about, so writing and checking it is still analysis-of-the-model work, not model-authoring in the SA-3/SA-8 sense -- no chapter's own construct count changes, matching how `allocate` usages already recur across chapters without each counting as new (design spec SS2). This reading was never separately escalated; it followed directly from the already-confirmed F4/SA-8 precedent and needed no new ACE ruling.
Known gaps recorded here, not fixed, for a future pass (none block this entry; none were silently dropped):
  - The cross-representation asymmetry above (`src/toaster/evidence.py` `validate_record`), Task 5's reviewer.
  - A judgment-record notebook's own construction-zone group count (five vs. seven, whether it introduces a new tag or only reconstructs) is now documented in `toaster-review-protocol` but not yet reflected as its own named convention anywhere else; minor, cosmetic.
  - `chapters/ch02-requirements/index.md`/`conclusion.md` do not mention the new `ReviewRecordRef` construct Ch2 now introduces; Task 3's reviewer (Q2).
  - `models/ch02-cumulative.sysml`'s own `// Source: notebook cell-02 TOASTER_INCREMENT...` header comment leaves out the judgment notebook's own later increment; Task 3's reviewer (Q3).
  - `subject_ref` and `model_ref` carry the same value with no sentence distinguishing their roles in AC-001's own notebook; Task 3's reviewer (Q4).
  - `toaster-recipe`'s general chapter-notebook seam rule says "exactly one sentence"; several judgment-record seam cells (Ch8 nb02, Ch6 nb03) carry two, prepending the new bridged-connection sentence to an already-accurate original one rather than deleting real content; Task 6's and Task 11's reviewers, both accepted as a reasonable reading, not reconciled into a single stated rule.
  - `.claude/skills/toaster-review-protocol/SKILL.md`'s own worked example still cites `model_ref="models/ch07-snapshot.sysml"`, a file that does not exist (pre-existing, confirmed unrelated to this work); Task 10's reviewer, Finding B.
  - A pre-existing, repo-wide `SS` -> `§` section-symbol mojibake (`SS7.21.1`/`SS7.21.2`, confirmed present in `chapters/ch10-traceability-signoff/index.md`, `conclusion.md`, and a notebook premise string, all predating this session's own work from the earlier energy-conservation-tie pass) was found and deliberately NOT touched, out of scope for this plan; the instance this session itself introduced (in its own plan document, then copied verbatim into `evidence.py`, `query.py` and `toaster-recipe/SKILL.md`) was found and fixed.
  - Two commits on this branch (`ca1b460`, and the original, now-superseded `04820d7`) carry a `Co-Authored-By` trailer, violating this repo's own stated no-trailer convention; a message-only cleanup is safe (nothing from this branch has been pushed to `origin/pass1/harness-alignment`) but was not performed here -- attempting a multi-commit history rewrite to fix it was blocked by this session's own auto-mode git-safety policy ("Git Destructive"), and the policy was respected rather than routed around.
Provenance: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-hawkins-judgment-record-anchor-design.md; docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-hawkins-judgment-record-anchor-plan.md and its own in-flight amendment (commit c8ccef7, adding Task 15 and the Task 14 notebook-execution sweep after Task 1's review found both gaps); `src/toaster/evidence.py`, `src/toaster/query.py`; `models/ch02-cumulative.sysml` through `ch08-cumulative.sysml`, `ch10-cumulative.sysml`; `chapters/ch02-requirements/03-judgment-context.ipynb`, `ch03-measures/01-moe-definition.ipynb`, `ch03-measures/03-threshold-judgment.ipynb`, `ch04-functional-decomp/03-completeness-check.ipynb`, `ch06-recursive-decomp/02-second-level.ipynb`, `ch06-recursive-decomp/03-stopping-judgment.ipynb`, `ch08-checking/02-violation-witness.ipynb`, `ch08-checking/03-revision-flow.ipynb`, `ch09-coverage-sufficiency/02-evidence-completeness.ipynb`, `ch09-coverage-sufficiency/03-stale-detection.ipynb`, `ch10-traceability-signoff/01-traceability-graph.ipynb`, `ch10-traceability-signoff/02-judgment-synthesis.ipynb`, `ch10-traceability-signoff/03-engineering-signoff.ipynb`; `exercises/ch02/exercise.ipynb` through `exercises/ch10/exercise.ipynb` (seven touched); `.claude/skills/toaster-review-protocol/SKILL.md`, `.claude/skills/toaster-recipe/SKILL.md`; `glossary/definitions/tutorial.ttl`, `docs/glossary.md`; `scripts/check_construction.py`; `decisions/work-contract-template.md` (the review contract this execution followed); Hawkins, Kelly, Knight and Graydon 2011 (SSS 2011, pp. 3-23, the authors' own self-archived copy, sha256-verified read in full); SysML v2 formal/2026-03-02 SS7.27.2; a live OpenSysML v0.9.0 probe (this session, re-confirming the design's own earlier probe) establishing the exact JSON shapes `get_review_record_refs()` depends on; DL-075 through DL-083 (the escalation this closes and its nine prior occurrences).

## DL-085 | 2026-10-01 | ACE-GRID-SYNTHESIS | First persona x modality grid run (10 cells) synthesized: zero blocking content defects; two exercise-scaffolding clusters escalated to Z; stale-worktree provisioning confirmed a third time and ruled; four stale-base findings voided as false alarms

Path: Handled by the ACE (methodology rulings; `model_ref`; the coverage-helper limitation; the static-build link check; voiding the stale-base findings) / escalated to Z (exercise first-run convention; exercise carry-forward) / batched for Z's one-shot direction (five minor and cosmetic items, per `user-testing` skill step 3: minor and cosmetic items are not decided without Z).
Grid coverage: Ran: M1-novice (README, file tree); M2-novice (Ch1 index + nb01, Ch2 index only -- partial, budget exhausted; Ch1 nb02-04, conclusion and Ch2 sub-notebooks not read); M2-practitioner (Ch6, Ch8, all notebooks); M2-returning (Ch10, all notebooks + Ch9 conclusion); M3-novice (Ch2); M3-practitioner (Ch6); M3-returning (Ch10); M4-novice (Ch1 exercise); M4-practitioner (Ch8 exercise); M4-returning (Ch10 exercise). Not run by design: M1-practitioner, M1-returning. Compromised by a stale worktree base (provisioned off `main`/`120c65d`/`2ceedac`, none of which carries DL-084's retrofit; `120c65d` is not an ancestor of this branch's `HEAD` at `62dcd5e`): M3-novice, M3-practitioner, M3-returning, M4-practitioner -- only their claims about `subject_ref`/`ReviewRecordRef` are void; every other finding from those cells tested content identical between the stale base and `HEAD` (orchestrator-verified by `git cat-file -p` diff at each base) and stands. M4-returning hit a related branch mismatch and correctly worked around it (read the design spec via `git show`); its findings are not compromised. The design doc says "nine cells" but its own table marks ten; ten ran.
Decision:
  (1) Ruled, methodology. Worktree provisioning for a grid cell follows the rule `.claude/agents/orchestrator.md` already states (line 17: create the worktree yourself with `git worktree add <path> -b <branch> <current-branch>`; never rely on the harness's own isolation mechanism, per `decisions/cold-start.md`) -- this session's own grid dispatch did not follow it, a process miss, not a new gap. Going forward, every M3/M4-class contract records its own dispatch commit and requires a report line `Base check: git merge-base --is-ancestor <dispatch-commit> HEAD -> yes/no`; a "no" voids the cell instead of being silently reported around. `.claude/skills/user-testing/SKILL.md` line 69's execution command hardcodes `cd /Users/z/Documents/GitHub/toaster`, which defeats worktree isolation by reading notebook text from the main checkout regardless of which worktree a cell actually runs in -- the direct cause of the one false NEEDS-FIX below; fixed to reference the notebook's own directory in the agent's own worktree. The interview step (the design's own new ACE capability) is infeasible for an ACE run as a subagent -- sibling grid cells are not addressable from its process (confirmed: `SendMessage` to "M3-novice" failed, "No agent named 'M3-novice' is reachable"); routed through the existing escalation chain instead (orchestrator.md line 12): the ACE names the cell and the question in its report, the orchestrator forwards it and resumes the ACE by name with the answer. Untested alternative, not adopted here: passing cells' raw agent ids to the ACE directly.
  (2) Voided as false alarms (stale base, not content): M3-novice's own NEEDS-FIX verdict (the `ReviewRecordRef` gap it reported does not exist on this branch -- at `HEAD` `chapters/ch02-requirements/03-judgment-context.ipynb` carries 11 occurrences, `models/ch02-cumulative.sysml` 2, `src/toaster/evidence.py` 12 of `subject_ref`; at `2ceedac`, `120c65d` and `main` all three are 0, confirmed by direct `git cat-file -p` reads at each candidate base). Because no candidate base carries the notebook text M3-novice quoted, that text was read from the main checkout while the model file came from the stale worktree -- a mixed-path read, caused by the hardcoded `cd` in (1), not a content defect. Also voided on the same basis: three further `subject_ref`/anchor claims from M3-practitioner and M3-returning. Ch2's own prior checkpoint verdict (DL-075 context) is unaffected; M3-novice's own execution results were otherwise consistent with PASS.
  (3) Ruled: M3-practitioner's finding that `ReviewRecord.model_ref` is never resolved against the loaded model by `validate_record()` is confirmed accurate by direct read (`src/toaster/evidence.py`: `model_ref` appears only as a field declaration, never referenced in `validate_record`'s own logic) but superseded by DL-084, applied as a prior decision: `subject_ref` is the element anchor and IS resolved (`model.find(r.subject_ref)`, evidence.py's own resolution block); `model_ref` plus `content_hash` identify the whole source for `check_stale()`, a different mechanism by design, not a gap in the new one. No new action; the residual (no sentence in AC-001's own notebook distinguishing the two roles) is already DL-084's own recorded known gap (Q4).
  (4) Ruled: M4-returning's coverage-helper snag is a real, undocumented limitation, not a content defect -- confirmed by direct read: `requirement_coverage()` (`src/toaster/query.py`) keys on the qualified name of a `satisfy` relationship's own `subject` element, so a chained feature subject (`assert not satisfy X by a.b`) never appears under `a`. No chapter uses a chained subject (every taught form is a top-level usage), so tutorial content is unaffected. Tracked (not fixed as an urgent item): one docstring line added to `requirement_coverage`/`satisfy_relationships` recording the limitation, to be picked up by the next contract that touches `query.py`.
  (5) Ruled: M2-novice's exercise link (`chapters/ch01-system-purpose/index.md` line 40, `../../exercises/ch01/exercise.ipynb`) leaves the MyST-built book for a bare Jupyter server on the dev preview, because `myst.yml` excludes `exercises/**` from the build; on the static GitHub Pages build the link's behavior is unverified and plausibly dead. Added to the CI/CD deploy-readiness plan's own acceptance criteria: every chapter `index.md`/`conclusion.md` exercise link must resolve on the built static site (rewrite to the file's GitHub URL if MyST cannot serve an excluded path).
  (6) Escalated to Z (Brief A, below): exercise first-run-unmodified convention. Verified by direct read and by the ACE's own `nbconvert` execution of the unmodified notebook: `exercises/ch01`-`ch07` print `Model ok`/`Step N ok` and continue past an unfilled step; `exercises/ch08` prints at its first cell then halts two cells later with an uncaught `AssertionError` naming a missing model element, with no gate at the point of failure; `exercises/ch09`/`ch10` gate immediately at their first cell with an `assert model.ok` naming what to paste in. Severity: CONFUSING, not blocking -- nothing prevents progress once the fill-in step is done, but a learner without Chapter 1 to compare against (M4-practitioner's own point, independently corroborating M4-practitioner's earlier Ch8-cold-run report) reads the mixed halt as a broken exercise rather than an intentional one.
  (7) Escalated to Z (Brief B, below): exercise carry-forward. Every exercise's own first cell instructs "paste your completed Ch(N-1) model here"; nothing in the repo checkpoints that state anywhere, and `docs/setup.md`'s own fork-and-exercise section says nothing about keeping it between chapters. Ch10's own exercise additionally needs two distinct Ch6 intermediate states (M4-returning). M4-practitioner's two new Ch8 friction points (an unqualified `DimensionOneValue`; an `SI::'kg/s'` literal that does not resolve, the real unit being `SI::'kg⋅s⁻¹'`, confirmed against `SI.sysml` line 196) are a symptom of this same cause, not a Ch8 teaching gap: `exercises/ch07/exercise.ipynb`'s own first cell states the `DimensionOneValue` import a learner needs, and `exercises/ch08/exercise.ipynb`'s own first cell names the correct unit literal -- both are missed only by a learner who starts Ch8 cold, without the Ch7 exercise model the notebook assumes they are pasting in.
  (8) Batched for Z, not decided (minor/cosmetic, per `user-testing` skill step 3): a clause in Ch8 nb02 cell 33 that echoes the evaluator's own seam criterion, attached to DL-084's known gap on two-sentence seam cells, not re-ruled; concept-statement density in Ch10 nb01 and Ch6, attached to DL-077(2)/DL-083, not re-ruled; the README quick-start naming `uv`/`mystmd` without saying what they are, though `docs/setup.md` already lists both as prerequisites with links (optional one-line README pointer); `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`/`DEFERRED.md` at the repo root reading as internal to a first-time visitor (out of scope, the repo's own working contract, Z's call); Ch10's own two-Ch6-state exercise requirement (folded into Brief B's option C).
  (9) Recorded, no action: the BASE_URL warning banner M2-practitioner reported (orchestrator could not reproduce it on a clean load); a sidebar-overlap observation M2-returning attributed to its own viewport emulation; a sidebar click-behavior note from M2-novice -- re-check all three against the static build once it exists. M2-novice's own partial coverage (budget exhausted before finishing Ch1/Ch2) is a coverage gap for the next run, not a finding about the book; scope a Haiku-tier M2 cell to one chapter next time. Most reports omit the model they actually ran on, which `.claude/agents/simulated-learner.md` line 30 requires; enforce this at dispatch.
  (10) Positive, do not touch: the rendered book (Ch1, 2, 6, 8, 10) and the local chapters (Ch2, 6, 10) -- no rendering or execution defects found, every negative control fired as stated, every seam addressed in behavior and never named (six cells evaluated it), DL-084's own anchor verified working on the real branch by two independent cells (M2-practitioner on Ch6/Ch8, M2-returning on Ch10, including the AI-C10 exemption actually exercised), Ch9-to-Ch10 continuity confirmed by two personas, the Ch1 exercise completable cold by a Haiku-tier novice (M4-novice), and the README clone flow (M1-novice). Zero blocking content defects across all ten cells.
