## DL-100 | 2026-10-02 | USER-TESTING-NOVICE-BROWSER | Longitudinal novice browser test (all 10 chapters, live site): zero blocking findings; ACE ruled one P4 violation, escalated the rest

Path: Handled by ACE (one ruling: S13) / Escalated to Z (structural and minor items)
Decision: A single continuous Novice persona (Haiku 4.5, per `user-testing`'s own model-pinning rule) read all 54 pages of the live MyST site end to end via its own navigation; the ACE (Fable 5.1) then interviewed that persona directly across three real rounds of questioning, did its own first-hand spot-check of the page the persona called worst (Ch6-02 `/second-level`), and triaged every finding. Result: zero BLOCKING findings. The persona's own "Understood" bullets correctly summarize every page it visited, including the two it initially rated 5/5 "catastrophic" -- both were withdrawn to 3.5-4/5 under questioning once fatigue was separated from the page's actual content, and neither carries a construct new to the reader. Ruled: S13 -- a builder-facing skill-file citation ("(architecture-layers skill)") had leaked into the learner-facing `criteria` field of two judgment records (`chapters/ch03-measures/01-moe-definition.ipynb`, `chapters/ch06-recursive-decomp/02-second-level.ipynb`); this is a clear-cut P4 violation, not a teaching-approach judgment call, so the ACE ruled directly (replace with "(SEBoK's MoE/MoP distinction, PDF 1562)", matching this tutorial's own existing citation style) and the orchestrator dispatched the fix through the normal builder/reviewer contract pipeline (branch `usertest/fix-s13`). Escalated to Z, per three options (A: glossary affordance only -- links at first use plus three new terms (conjugated port, feature chain, judgment record) plus one framing paragraph on Ch2's overview; B: A plus restructuring Ch6 so no sub-notebook carries two full records; C: A plus re-running the novice test with intact inline-code reading before any prose change). ACE recommends C.
Principles applied: P4 (builder-facing lenses never in learner content); F5 (cite the canonical kind of definition asked for); P1, SA-7 (full worked-example judgment records, by design); P3 (figures as judged views); P6 (Z keeps substantive, learning-outcome-affecting decisions); the `user-testing` skill's own severity scale and "do not decide minor or cosmetic items without Z's direction" rule.
Reasoning: both of the persona's 5/5 ratings were re-examined by the ACE directly (23 cells on `/second-level`, not "50+"; ~12 cells on `/completeness-check`, not "40+" -- the persona had been counting rendered output blocks as code blocks) and both lacked any construct genuinely new to the reader; the persona itself conceded, under the ACE's questioning, that the ratings reflected cumulative fatigue from the preceding pages rather than that page's own content. Separately, the ACE found and verified a real methodological flaw in the persona's own reading method: its text extraction stripped inline `<code>` spans from rendered prose before judging it, so sentences that are complete and correct on the live page (verified directly by the ACE at `/assumptions` and `/interfaces`) were read by the persona as "cut off mid-sentence." This weakens every prose-clarity finding in the diary specifically, while leaving the structural/pacing/density findings (which don't depend on reading inline code) intact -- this asymmetry is why the ACE recommends re-running before any prose rewrite (option C), rather than acting on the prose complaints now. S13 was ruled rather than escalated because it is mechanical: the fix makes nothing harder to learn and the citation refers to a file the learner has no access to, the same class of violation as prior Tall-lens-vocabulary leaks this project has already ruled on without escalation.
Determined: yes for the S13 ruling and the zero-blocking verdict; the structural/minor items (whether to add glossary affordances, whether to restructure Ch6, whether to re-run the test first) are underdetermined at P6 (they change a learning outcome or a structural choice, which is Z's to make).
Extension: yes -- P4 (builder-facing material excluded from learner content) has previously been applied only to the Tall-lens vocabulary rule; here it is extended to a citation of a builder-facing skill file appearing inside a judgment record's own field value.
Provenance: `decisions/user-testing/longitudinal-novice-browser-diary.md` (54 page-by-page entries plus final synthesis, branch `usertest/novice-browser`); `decisions/user-testing/ace-interview-notes.md` (three interview rounds, spot-check, triage table, decision-log draft, branch `usertest/ace-interview`); `decisions/user-testing/longitudinal-novice-browser-synthesis.md` (orchestrator's combined report); `.claude/skills/user-testing/SKILL.md` (severity scale, ACE synthesis protocol); glossary CLI lookups for `mechanism`, `MoE`, `MoP`, `allocation`, `abstract definition`, `interface`, `judgment`, `asserted context` (entries exist) and `conjugation`, `feature chain`, `judgment record` (none); DL-037 (records-before-mechanism-suggestive-names, cited by the ACE's spot-check of `/second-level`); branch `usertest/fix-s13` (the S13 fix, dispatched through the standard contract pipeline).

## DL-103 | 2026-10-02 | USER-TESTING-NOVICE-BROWSER-RERUN | Skill edit (user-testing): evidence rules for browser read-through diaries, from the round-1 and round-2 error pattern

Status: COMPLETE (2026-10-02). Post-edit check: the new subsection sits between the unchanged "Execution command" passage and the unchanged "## Report format" section; the execution checklist above is explicitly kept in force ("the checklist above still applies"); no adjacent rule is weakened; `uv run python -m glossary check` passes (0 errors, 7 pre-existing source-absent warnings); the subsection introduces no glossary term and nothing in it bears on AGENTS.md Part 1. Summary: added four binding evidence rules for browser read-through diaries (get_page_text for prose, minimum per-page evidence, string-level verification of quoted defects, one reader or a hand-off marker) because rounds 1 and 2 each produced findings that these rules would have prevented. Noted, not changed (separate logical change, needs its own entry): this skill's "ACE synthesis protocol" step 3 says the ACE "does not edit repository files", which predates and is narrower than `ace-protocol`'s skill-modification authority under which this edit was made.
Path: Handled by ACE (skill modification under `ace-protocol` "Skill modification authority" and `skill-editor` "tighter prohibition derived from an observed error pattern"; the orchestrator asked for it after DL-102; DL-102 itself is on the orchestrator's branch, which is why this entry follows DL-101 here)
Decision: Add one short, additive subsection to `.claude/skills/user-testing/SKILL.md`, between the "Execution command" block and "## Report format", stating four binding evidence rules for any browser-based read-through diary: (1) `get_page_text` is the required primary reading tool for prose (the accessibility tree from `read_page` truncates long text nodes and renders inline-code spans as separate child nodes, the likely cause of round 1's inline-code-stripping defect, DL-100); (2) a minimum evidence requirement per page before an observation is written (the page opened in the browser, its text extracted, every figure screenshotted, any quoted sentence copied from the extracted text, not from memory); (3) any reported defect that names a specific string, caption, output or number must be verified string-level against the extracted page text before it is written, since page-level verification does not catch string-level fabrication (DL-102, R2-1 and R2-2); (4) one reader per diary, or an explicit hand-off marker in the diary naming the page range each agent instance read, so the ACE can weight interview testimony (DL-102, M4). No existing rule is removed, reworded or reordered.
Principles applied: P5 (do not paper over: a learning about method is recorded durably in the repo, the same session); P1 (evidence cited before a claim is made applies to the test method as much as to the book); `skill-editor` Step 2 (no prohibition removed, one archetype's primary skill, no learning outcome affected: unilateral), Step 3 (additive, well under 20% of the skill).
Reasoning: Round 1's prose complaints were artifacts of a reading method that dropped inline code (DL-100); round 2 fixed that with `get_page_text` and the complaints vanished (DL-102), which is the evidence that the tool choice is the fix. Round 2 then showed two further holes the skill does not close: a reported caption mismatch and a doubled qualified name on `/part-def` that do not exist on the page or in its source, surviving page-level verification; and a diary written at about one minute per page by more than one agent instance, whose interview testimony could not say which pages it had read. Each rule below is the smallest prohibition that would have caught the observed error. The change is one logical change (evidence rules for one test mode), so the one-change-per-session limit holds.
Determined: yes.
Extension: no.
Provenance: DL-100 (round-1 extraction defect), DL-101 (Z's choice to re-run), DL-102 (round-2 triage, items R2-1, R2-2, M1 to M4), `decisions/user-testing/ace-interview-notes-r2.md` sections 2, 3 and 3b (first-hand `/part-def` check; diary commit history showing reader hand-offs at pages 19, 29 and 43 and the one-minute-per-page pace).
Revert record (verbatim current text of the region being changed; the new subsection is inserted into the blank line between these two existing passages, which are themselves unchanged):
    A worktree-isolated cell that runs from the main checkout's path instead of its own worktree reads
    some files (e.g. notebook text) from the wrong branch state while reading others (e.g. model files)
    from its own worktree — a mixed-path read that produces false findings. Confirmed as the cause of a
    false NEEDS-FIX verdict in the first grid run (`decisions/log.md` DL-085).

    ## Report format
  Revert: delete the inserted subsection so that the blank line between "DL-085)." and "## Report format" is restored.
